THE DOCKET · Amenti Engineering Brief
· 

Engineering Brief·The Shelf·Ingram Manor LLC

11 August 2026·2026-08-11·Rev. B

A Paper That Covers Its Own Building

DOCKET

Cover: the gate at Amenti. A carved canyon that only admits it is carved where the light rakes across it, and one figure walking out toward something the size of a mountain.

The session began with an instruction to harvest museum plates for article banners. It ended with a court. Nothing in between was a change of plan — each turn was a measurement that contradicted the thing before it, and the last of them found that the strongest surface in the application was the one nobody had built yet.

EVERY FIGURE IN THIS DOCUMENT WAS READ FROM A LIVE WORKER
AT THE TIME OF WRITING. NONE OF THEM IS REMEMBERED.

What changed, in one figure

The application entered this session as a site that generated its content when somebody looked at it and left it unillustrated. It leaves as one that publishes on a schedule, illustrates itself, and reports on its own court.

The session in one frame. Seven rows; six of them were not on the list when it started.

The route, and why it kept turning

A brief that reports only the destination hides the thing worth knowing. Every turn below was a correction, and the correction came from the captain each time. They are recorded because the reasoning is more reusable than the result.

Turn 01The Met, measured and abandonedClosed

The standing instruction was to harvest CC0 museum plates. Four premises in the previous handoff failed within ten minutes of testing — the date filter returns nothing on narrow BCE bands, departmentId collapses a result set, departmentId is not in the object payload at all, and department 3 had been renamed.

What actually stopped it
Not supply — narrative. Subject tags exist and are the right signal, but coverage collapses outside Greek and Roman: 0 of 45 sampled objects tagged in both Medieval and Ancient West Asian. Period matching and narrative content pull against each other and the collection will not give you both.

Turn 02Scenes, keyed to the articleShipped

The obvious replacement was one generated image per figure. That is the restriction, not the solution. Shaka filed two dispatches on consecutive days about different subjects; one image per figure is one image for every article that figure will ever write.

The article knows what it is about. The author key does not.

Turn 03Automation, taken literallyShipped

The first design had 64 images generated by hand and named to match their tags. That is a human matching pictures to words — and it was a design choice, not a constraint. The Worker renders its own.

Turn 04The courtFront page live

The Dispatch was seven figures writing on one theme, which is a magazine. A court paper covers its own building — and the building was already live, with 59 cases, a verdicts table and an argument feed, none of it exposed.

The scene pipeline

Scenes are situations, not figures: burning-library, empty-senate, narrow-pass, moonlit-night. Sixty-four of them. One burning library serves Thoth on Alexandria and Hypatia, which per-figure keying could never do.

Render once per tag, ever. The same shape as the TTS archive already in production.

DISTANT AND UNPEOPLED IS NOT AN AESTHETIC PREFERENCE.
A SILHOUETTE HAS NO LIKENESS TO GET WRONG — SO A BAD MATCH IS A DULL IMAGE
RATHER THAN AN EMBARRASSING ONE. THAT IS WHAT MAKES IT SAFE TO RUN UNWATCHED.

It is the exact inverse of the character portraits, which need the 198×160 preview discipline because the face is the subject. The two pipelines look similar and have opposite tolerances.

What the model actually chose

Thirty-five distinct tags across 77 essays and 18 dispatches — the vocabulary is being used, not collapsing onto a handful.

Selection quality, sampled. The failure this guards against is a pool where one tag absorbs everything.

Publishing: from a visitor's click to a schedule

Atlantica was generated on page load — not on a click, not on a cron. The evidence is in the dates rather than the code.

The diagnosis came from the shape of the data, not from reading the handler.

THE ARTICLE SHOULD EXIST BEFORE A READER ARRIVES,
NOT BECAUSE ONE DID.

Seventy-seven finished essays became a queue: publishAt on every one, three a week, author-spaced so no voice files twice in a week. Twenty-six weeks of publishing that costs nothing to serve, because every word already existed.

Publication Was Is Runway 

Atlantica generate-on-read queued, 3/week 26 weeks 

Amenti Dispatch never ran front page + 7 features weekly 

VALL·HALLA held held — deliberately gated 

The Daily Planet was renamed to the Amenti Dispatch. The old name was Superman's newspaper on a site about Gilgamesh — the one element that read as pastiche rather than as the thing itself.

The covers

An image with a title on it is a poster. What lifts an essay out of a .md file is the type: a hook large enough to carry an argument, and a line lifted from the piece set small beneath it. The scene sits behind, dimmed, and never competes.

All three — sceneTag, hook, pull — are derived from the essay's own text in one call per article. The essays are not rewritten. The model is asked only to point at what is already in them.

Akhenaten · "The Heresy of Shadows: On the Necessity of One Light"

hook Every theology before him was a system of brokerage;
he dismantled it by pointing at the sun.
pull The sun does not require an agent.
scene temple-steps

One of 77. Zero failures across the backfill.

What the audit found

A read-only probe walks the paths a reader walks and checks twenty-two invariants against live data. It is GET only by construction — no writes, no generation, no auth, no votes.

THE COST WALL CANNOT TELL A LOAD TEST FROM AN ATTACK.
DAILY_TOKENS IS 2,000,000. TRIPPING IT TAKES THE SITE DOWN FOR REAL READERS
UNTIL 00:00 UTC. A PROBE THAT CAN SPEND CAN CAUSE THE OUTAGE IT MEASURES FOR.

It found, on its first proper run, a live exposure that had been open and unnoticed:

Finding · HIGH · ORIGIN_OPEN

A request bearing Origin: https://not-amenti.example was accepted. ALLOWED_ORIGINS had never been set, so the allowlist fell through to * while the code reported that it had checked. Any website could have spent the Anthropic and Gemini keys from a visitor's browser.

The Worker's own cost-wall header predicted this exactly and said to run wrangler secret list. Nobody had. The probe is what finally checked.

Closing it took two attempts, and the failed one is the more instructive:

Origin sent Before First fix Now 

https://amenti.live 200 200 200 

(no Origin header) 200 403 ← site went dark 200 

https://not-amenti.example 200 ← exposed 403 403 

https://amenti.live.evil.com 200 ← exposed 403 403 

http://amenti.live 200 403 403 

A browser sends Origin on cross-origin requests and on POSTs. A same-origin GET sends nothing at all — which is how the site's own feed calls arrive. Treating absent as hostile blanked both publications. Absent is now allowed; wrong is still refused. Nothing is given away: a client that sends no Origin is not a browser, and the caps were always what stopped a person with curl.

Performance: the bug that looked fine

/feed?details=1 did one KV read per record. Nothing in the code looked wrong.

Sixty-fold on the cold path. The index is keyed by day, because publishAt changes the visible set at midnight with no write at all.

Under load

Path 4 readers 12 readers 25 readers Cache 

atlantica feed 55 ms 57 ms 55 ms 100% 

dispatch feed 57 ms 72 ms 56 ms 100% 

article 62 ms 64 ms 61 ms n/a 

Flat from 4 to 25. A path whose median rises with reader count is contended; one that stays level is cached or cheap.

A clean 5,000–5,200 ms ceiling appeared across unrelated paths including the mint — but only from a single IP running 25 threads, and never through a Worker. That is a limiter doing its job against a client that looks like an attacker, not an application limit. Recorded so it is not mistaken for one later.

The court

Everything a newsroom needs was already in the database and none of it was readable without the admin secret. GET /hall exposes the aggregates and withholds every identifying field.

Published Withheld 

docket · 59 cases, figure, era, domain user_id 

verdicts · figure, topic, votes, week display_name 

arguments · text and vote count author identity 

pool · rank, votes, projected payout balances, handles 

emeralds · minted, burned, circulating profile rows 

An argument is reported by its text and its votes — the part that was public the moment it was posted. Who wrote it is not the story and is not ours to publish.

What a case looks like when it is drawn

Arthur and the Round Table — case 3 of 59 on the live docket.

A king seated at a table while riders come in over the hill is the summons made literal, and nothing in the pipeline was told to make it so. The scene tag was chosen by the same call that wrote the piece; the figure was chosen by the docket.

It is also the argument for tagging situations rather than figures. The riders are approaching and the king has not stood up. That is a case being called, and it would serve any figure summoned to answer for something — which per-figure keying could never do.

Who brings the charges

This was the open question of the session, and the answer was already in the source text rather than needing invention.

IN THE WEIGHING OF THE HEART THERE IS NO PROSECUTOR.
THE HEART TESTIFIES AGAINST ITSELF — THE DEAD MAN EVEN PRAYS IT WILL NOT.
THE STANDARD IS A FEATHER, AND A FEATHER CANNOT BE ARGUED WITH.

Which is stronger than an invented accuser: you can dislike a prosecutor and side with the accused out of sympathy. You cannot dislike a scale.

The third voice

The court had two voices and needed three. Anubis summons and heightens. Thoth records and stops. Nobody spoke for the accused.

In Spell 125 the dead man speaks first, before any charge, because there is no charge and no prosecutor. What he makes is not a plea but a declaration — addressed to forty-two assessors each named, and almost entirely about small things.

I HAVE NOT DIMINISHED FROM THE BUSHEL.
I HAVE NOT TAKEN MILK FROM THE MOUTHS OF CHILDREN.
I HAVE NOT STOPPED WATER WHEN IT SHOULD FLOW.

That register is what makes the slot safe to build. A confession that let a figure deny what their own reading room documents would turn the paper into an exculpation machine and undo every honest note in the library — Jefferson's room says he freed two people of six hundred; Columbus's says he kept two logbooks; Shaka's says his record was written by a man paid to make him terrible.

The declaration never touches the great crime. It works through the ordinary conduct of a life, and the rule is absolute: affirm only what is true, and where an item cannot be said, leave it unsaid and say so in one sentence. No defence, no explaining that the times were different.

THE OMISSIONS ARE THE PIECE.
THE TEXT CLOSES "I AM PURE" FOUR TIMES.
A FIGURE WHO REACHES THAT LINE HAVING PASSED OVER FIVE ITEMS
HAS SAID MORE THAN ANY DENIAL COULD.

What was built and then removed

The first draft of the declaration stored a count — passed: 5 — of the items a figure could not affirm. It was wrong and it is gone.

Nothing measures that number. A model writes the declaration and reports its own arithmetic about a real person's moral record, and the site renders it as data. The library refuses this everywhere: Josephus's disputed passages are flagged, not resolved; Jefferson's room says outright that it does not attempt to reconcile. A count reconciles silently and looks quantitative.

"I CANNOT SAY I HAVE MADE NO ONE TO WEEP" IS A COMPOSITION IN A VOICE.
"5 OF 20" IS FICTION WEARING THE COSTUME OF DATA.

The charge, which nobody writes

A charge implies someone brought it, and this court has no prosecutor. The resolution is that there is no neutral sentence. All three of these are true, describe the same act, and take opposite sides:

Caesar crossed a river with an army he was ordered to disband. 

Caesar ended a republic that had been dying for fifty years. 

Caesar chose civil war over standing trial. 

A prose charge acquires an advocate the moment it acquires a verb. But a filing is not a sentence — caesar-rubicon · Caesar at the Rubicon · ROMAN ERA · 49 BC · War & Strategy · Ambition & Its Cost locates without claiming, and it already exists in /hall. Nothing has to write it.

It rides on the summons rather than beside it as a fourth article. Anubis is rhetoric by design; the filing is what a reader who distrusts him can check against. Keeping it inside also holds the court to three slots of eleven — 27% of the issue rather than 33%.

SUMMONS · CHARGE · DECLARATION · REGISTER
THE ORDER OF SPELL 125, AND NOW THE ORDER OF THE PAPER.
NONE OF THE FOUR DECIDES ANYTHING.

Ammit was the one piece missing. She is now the roster's 1,008th figure.

The front page

Anubis previews the week's case, rotating all 59 with a coprime stride — no case repeats for 59 weeks. The first version preferred cases already in session, which sounded right and returned Caesar at the Rubicon every week forever, because exactly one case is open. Caught by simulation before it shipped.

SENSATIONALISE THE STAKES. NEVER THE VERDICT.
THE PAPER REPORTS THE RESULT NEXT WEEK AND CANNOT BE SEEN
TO HAVE MANUFACTURED IT.

ANUBIS · front page · Sun Tzu and the Concubines

"Was the drill ground massacre discipline — or murder?"

Two readings, both argued at full force. What the verdict would MEAN.
The deadline, pulled from settlesAt. And no hint of a preferred answer.

The stagger

Each strand a full turn behind the one it reads. The rule is a state, not a duration: it reads a hall that sealed long ago, and it cannot read a half-open one.

Wednesday, not Saturday. The gap after publication is not staleness — it is the voting window.

Verdict → publication wants to be short (old news). Publication → next seal wants to be long (that is when readers act). Wednesday gives a 60-hour-old verdict and four and a half days of participation.

But the clock is the comfort, not the mechanism. The herald refuses to send unless the week manifest reads status: published and done === total. An email cannot be unsent, and a chunked run's likely failure is a half issue — which the old empty-issue guard would have mailed without complaint.

Findings that looked fine and were not

What it looked like What it was Cost had it shipped 

Feed returning correctly 77 KV reads for 2 KB 6.3 s per page load 

Teasers rendering Written hooks overwritten by body text paid for, discarded 

Weekly records dated All stamped Sunday matched 1 day in 7, silently 

Cron would publish max: 99 vs a ~50 subrequest ceiling half issue, mailed 

Origin allowlist checking Fell through to * keys spendable by any site 

Worker reaching the mint workers.dev loops back internally front page silently absent 

EVERY ROW ABOVE WAS INVISIBLE FROM READING THE CODE.
ALL SIX WERE FOUND BY MEASURING SOMETHING.

What this session put on the shelf

Thoth's reading room — Budge's Book of the Dead, nine works, Project Gutenberg #7145, public domain. Budge titles his fourth chapter "Thoth, the Author of the Book of the Dead", so the attribution is sourced rather than asserted.

Chapter CXXV is the centre of the shelf, and the fit is uncanny: the Hall of Maati, Osiris presiding, the Forty-Two assessors who "keep ward over sinners" on "the day of estimating characters", the balance, and Amemit — Budge's spelling — crouched beneath it.

The room note names Budge as a problem, not just a source: 1890s readings, superseded in places, Faulkner is the modern standard. Two earlier reading-room notes carried plausible facts absorbed from the wrong author. The shelf says plainly what it is.

Where it goes next

Open · in order

· Thoth's verdict slot — blocked until the court seals. Check whether the mint's cron is registered; empty verdicts and zero votes is the same signature the Dispatch showed before its missing trigger was found.

· The grid — the front page runs longer than the features and the featured slot needs to accommodate it.

· The crons — all still deliberately held. VALL·HALLA's own five-step release procedure still governs.

· Rooms for Osiris, Anubis, Ma'at, Ammit — the same Budge text cuts four more ways.

· The book — the docket cases as stories. Anubis calls the case, the figure testifies, Thoth records. The verdicts are real, cast by readers on the site. Nobody else can make that object. Blocked on the court actually sitting.

THE APPLICATION SPENT THIRTY HOURS BUILDING SURFACES
THAT PRODUCE HIGH-QUALITY OUTPUT. IT FINISHED BY BUILDING
THE ONE THAT PRODUCES THE BEST OF IT.

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC

Engineering Brief · Rev. B · 11 August 2026

All measurements read live. Nothing here is remembered.

THE AMENTI SPEECH DOCTRINE · The Matrix of Speech
· 

AMENTI.LIVE

Ingram Manor LLC · The Flying Dutchman

THE AMENTI SPEECH DOCTRINE

THE MATRIX OF SPEECH

Where the figure's judgment lives, why it kept escaping, and the law that keeps it home.
A record of the rewrite, the blunder in the middle, and the probe that caught it on the gangway.

12 JULY 2026 · NEW YORK HARBOR

Descend

PREFACEA PHILOSOPHY WITH NO HANDS

What this document is for

CONVERSATION-MATRIX.md is the doctrine in prose. It is the best writing in this fleet and nothing reads it. Not one line of it executes. It is a philosophy with no hands.

amenti-doctrine.js is the doctrine in data, and everything reads it.

This is the account of how the second came to exist, what it cost to learn, and — most usefully — the boundary error that produced the blunder in the middle. That error is subtle, it is easy to repeat, and it was caught by a question rather than by a test.

The purpose

This is not a triumphal record of a consolidation. It is a record of where doctrine lives, why it kept escaping, and the rule that keeps it home.

Read Section IV before you believe Section II

Everything described in THE REWRITE was written, tested by three hundred assertions — and was not running. The Terminal had never loaded the conversation core, and nothing in the fleet could see it.

That is not a footnote. It is the most important thing in this document, and it is why the last law reads: being loaded is not being used.

SECTION ITHE PREVIOUS STATE

What the system said before

The system listened for question marks

The old conversation core decided whether the figure was awaiting a reply by testing whether its own last sentence ended in a ?.

// amenti-chat.js, line 171 — the whole conversational model, in one regex
self._expecting = /\?\s*["')\]]*\s*$/.test(String(reply || ''));

That flag gated the microphone: an open expectation relaxed the input floor, so a bare "yes" would count as a turn.

It is a good design — for a conversation made of questions.

And it is the exact opposite of the Matrix, whose central law is statements, not questions. Because a question is a demand — it obliges the seeker to produce something, on the spot, in a shape you have chosen — and pressure makes people defend. Whereas a statement is an offer the seeker may correct, and correction is the cheapest form of disclosure that exists.

So the figure's most powerful moves carried no question mark at all —

"So it is the money that is the crux of it."

"You are not afraid of failing. You are afraid of being seen to have tried."

"Go on."
— every one of these is waiting. Not one ends in a question mark.

— and the system, reading punctuation, concluded the figure was not waiting.

The collision

The figure was awaiting an answer the machine did not know was coming. The mic gate, the length floor, and every timeout keyed to _expecting misfired. The Matrix could not run on the machine that was supposed to run it.

They are opposite models. The Matrix cannot be bolted on — the state machine has to learn to be interrupted.

Nobody could interrupt anybody

// line 148 — the seeker cannot speak while the figure thinks or talks
if (this.state === 'thinking' || this.state === 'speaking') return;

// line 212 — the mic opens ONLY from idle
armMic: function () { if (this.state !== 'idle') return; }

Strict turn-taking. The floor could not be taken, in either direction.

Which made the Arrest — the single most important instrument in the Matrix — structurally impossible. "Wait. Say that again." is an interruption. An arrest that arrives after the seeker has finished their paragraph is not an arrest. It is a delayed reaction — a system performing an attentiveness it did not have.

And in the other direction, during the Rendering, a seeker who wanted to say "no, that's not it" had to sit and wait politely for the figure to finish.

That is not counsel. That is a lecture.
Smooth turn-taking is the signature of a customer-service call.

And the reason it could not be interrupted was one missing function

The counsel did not speak through amenti-throttle.js. It spoke through AMENTI_VOICE — a fourth speech engine, inline in Page1.html, on no manifest, listed nowhere, a stowaway — and it had no stop() at all.

One absent method

Every grand claim in the Matrix about barge-in and the Arrest was standing on a speaker with no brakes. One missing function, and the entire real-time layer was fiction.

The system ejected the human for owning a television

The most quietly disgraceful thing in the old code:

breakdown 1 → "I'm not quite catching the thread — shall we slow down?"
breakdown 2 → "Still not hearing you clearly. Take your time."
breakdown 3 → "I think this isn't the moment — let's talk again soon." → onDisconnect()

Read those as a person would hear them. "Slow down." "Take your time." The system has concluded that you are the problem — and then it ends the conversation.

It never once considered that its own ear might be failing. A television in the room, a bad microphone, a noisy street — and the seeker was thrown out of the hall and blamed for it.

It was an ejection mechanism wearing a polite face.

SECTION IITHE REWRITE

What the figure now knows

The move tag — the keystone

Expectation is a property of intent, not of punctuation. Stop inferring it. Make the figure declare it.

[move: catch]
Wait. Say that again.

The core parses the tag, strips it before the screen and before the mouth, and _expecting is read, not guessed.

One declaration solves three problems. And if the model forgets the tag, the core falls back to punctuation — degrade, never break.

The instrument panel — tone as a probe

Tone is not decoration. A shift in temperature is a reading on a different axis.

register the instruction what the response reveals 

warm Speak gently, unhurried, with evident care. Do they open — or does warmth make them suspicious? Suspicion of kindness is a finding. 

cool Speak with clinical distance, level and unhurried. Relief, or abandonment? Some need the temperature dropped to speak at all. 

sharp Speak with sudden edge — clipped, direct, harder than before. Do they defend, collapse, or sharpen back? The one who sharpens back is not fragile. 

grave Speak slowly and heavily, as one who has paid for what he says. The counsel arrives with its price attached. 

danger Speak quietly, and let the quiet be worse than shouting. Retreat, or lean in? 

humour Let there be dry amusement in the voice, and something rueful under it. What a person laughs at is the most revealing datum available. Nothing else is close. 

The detectors — and an honest admission

The Arrest fires not on a heavy word — people say heavy things deliberately all the time — but on a heavy thing being discarded:

"The load-bearing thing is always buried in a subordinate clause and abandoned."

The weakest code in the fleet

ARREST_HEAVY. ARREST_DISMISS. ROOM_ASIDE. Hand-made English word lists carrying the most important moments in the product.

They will miss things. They will never catch "he doesn't call anymore" — no heavy word in it, and it is the entire conversation. No amount of cleverness fixes that. Only real seekers will.

The watchlist — the model arms the fast path

An arrest must land inside a beat. Human turn-taking gaps cluster near 200ms; past a second, an interruption becomes a comment on something you already finished saying.

So the trigger cannot ask a model. A round trip is one to two seconds. But it does not follow that the fast path must be stupid.

THE MODEL DOES NOT RUN ON THE FAST PATH.
IT ARMS THE FAST PATH.

// The figure has just read the whole conversation. It knows what THIS
// seeker buries — and it pre-writes the line, one turn early.
[move: reflect]
[watch: brother | the money]
[catch: Wait. Your brother. You have set him down twice now
and walked straight past him.]

So there is a distance there.

The intelligence arrives one turn early. The trigger stays instant.

The cap that was a confession

ARREST_MAX: 2 was never a design principle. It was a fear — a limit on the word list's error rate, wearing the costume of restraint. And a hard ceiling fails at the worst possible moment: the seeker on their third approach to the thing they are actually circling, when the arrest would finally matter — and the budget is spent on two lesser catches.

A cap that runs out is a cap that fails when it is needed.

arrestMaxList: 1 // the crude word list gets ONE strike, ever. I do not trust it.
arrestMaxWatch: 4 // the model-armed trigger is trusted further — because it is a
// BETTER TRIGGER, not because we became braver.

Rarity is an outcome, not a policy. The cooldown, the threshold and the clean-channel rule shape when it is right to reach. They do not run dry.

The Threshold — the first two minutes belong to an audience

The first minutes do not belong to a seeker. They belong to an audience, and the two are not the same person.

"holy crap" · "are you serious" · "no way" · "is this real" · "hey ma, come see this"

Every instrument in the fleet misfires there. Barge-in cuts the figure off mid-word on the first line anyone ever hears. The breakdown counter ejects the most delighted new user for being delighted. The Room hears a laugh of astonishment and asks "is that a dog?" The Arrest is a stranger grabbing your arm in the first ten seconds.

Not negotiable

Nothing sharp until they have crossed. One real exchange. THE FIGURE'S FIRST SENTENCE SURVIVES — that is the whole promise.

And do not suppress the shock. The shock is the product. A generic assistant has nowhere to stand when someone says "are you serious?" Caesar does.

The Room — presence, and the bright line

A person is on a call. Their child wanders in.
A human says "oh — hello!" A machine continues its sentence.
THAT GAP IS WHERE THE UNCANNY LIVES. NOT IN THE PROSE. IN THE NOT-NOTICING.

The signal was already in the audio: loud frames, and an empty transcript. Something made a noise that was not speech. The old code called that "a blip", counted it as a breakdown, and after three of them threw the human out.

A blip might be a dog.

The line

ACKNOWLEDGE WHAT ANNOUNCES ITSELF.
NEVER INVESTIGATE WHAT DOES NOT.

A dog barks → "Something is with you there. A dog, I would guess — am I wrong?" A near-miss in a party hat: warm, zero-risk, and now they are laughing about their dog and their defences are on the floor.

A faint voice in another room → it did not announce itself. "Who else is there?" is the exact moment hospitality becomes surveillance.

Three rules, in code, not in good intentions:

· The newcomer did not consent. Disclose what you are immediately, and gather nothing.

· "Just us" ends it instantly. Permanent. No second attempt, ever.

· NOTICE, DO NOT RECORD. The overheard words are never written to the transcript. Nothing a seeker said to their child is kept. Using it to be a better counsel in this hour is care. Storing it is a dossier.

The ear — the microphone is not a keyboard

A keyboard is DELIBERATE. A microphone is AMBIENT.

Every other input is chosen — the seeker decides what enters. The microphone accepts whatever is in the room, and the seeker does not choose what is in the room. That makes it the only untrusted input path in the fleet.

The ejection ladder is gone. In its place: the repair.

"I cannot hear you over that — there is too much noise where you are. Quiet it, and I will listen."
THE FIGURE REPORTS ITS OWN DIFFICULTY. IT NEVER ASKS WHAT THEY ARE DOING.

And one request. Then move house:

"My ear fails me here. Write to me instead — my eye does not."

Because not everyone can turn it down — a roommate, a street, a factory, a child. Someone told twice "I cannot hear you" who can do nothing about it has been politely excluded, and it will land as: the machine does not want to talk to me.

And the keyboard is not a lesser mode

send(text) never cared where the words came from. The escape hatch from the hardest problem in the system was the thing we had been standing on the entire time.

SECTION IIITHE FIRST BLUNDER

The boundary that was mislocated

What did NOT happen

There was no failed consolidation of the doctrine.

The doctrine was never together to begin with. There was nothing to consolidate. What existed was CONVERSATION-MATRIX.md — prose that nothing reads — some hand-written prompt strings inside amenti-chat.js, and nothing else.

Every move, register, detector and dial in this document was written in a single day. None of it existed that morning. It was created — and it was created scattered, across a dozen passes, each piece filed wherever it happened to be needed at the moment it was needed.

What DID happen: a boundary was mislocated

The engine consolidation succeeded. Four TTS engines — library.js, amenti-throttle.js, Page2.html, and the stowaway AMENTI_VOICE — became one. probe7 proves the R2 cache keys are byte-identical; the audio archive is a full hit; nothing re-renders.

And in the middle of that success, the doctrine was duplicated.

The register table was filed as machinery. So when the machinery consolidated, it went along — into two places.

The error, precisely

A CONVERSATIONAL JUDGMENT WAS FILED UNDER MACHINERY,
AND IT WENT WHEREVER THE MACHINERY WENT.

And the sting

On the same day, in the same codebase, a nine-page warning about exactly this was written.

composeStyle was hand-duplicated across library.js and amenti-throttle.js, with a comment in each warning that changing it "orphans the entire cached archive" — and nothing enforcing it. It was called, in writing:

A live financial tripwire with no tripwire on it.

Then probe7 was built to be the tripwire. And then another one was laid.

Why the boundary is subtle, and worth naming forever

MECHANISM JUDGMENT 

is how the figure speaks what the figure decides 

e.g. chunking · streaming · cancelling · cache keys · state machines when to be sharp · what counts as buried · how rare an arrest should be 

changes rarely, and it costs money to get wrong constantly, forever, as real people talk to it 

should be hard to change trivial to change 

The rule

CONSOLIDATING ONE WILL SCATTER THE OTHER —
UNLESS YOU NAME THE BOUNDARY FIRST.

We consolidated the pipes. The judgment rode along inside them and came out in two places.

SECTION IVTHE SECOND BLUNDER

The engine was never connected to the propeller

The first blunder was subtle: a judgment filed under machinery, which then travelled with the machinery into two places. It cost a duplicate.

The second is not subtle at all. It is total.

The finding

EVERYTHING IN SECTION II
WAS NEVER RUNNING.

Not the move tags. Not the registers. Not the Threshold. Not the Room. Not the mouth guard. Not the arrest, not the watchlist, not the Turn, not the doctrine.

The Terminal has never run amenti-chat.js. Not once. Not in its entire life.

The mechanism, in three lines

Page1's Terminal is an IIFE — (function(){ … })() — which executes at parse time, the moment the browser reaches it. And inside it, it asks for the conversation core:

// line 5989 — the Terminal's IIFE opens. This RUNS AS THE PARSER PASSES IT.
(function(){
try{
…
// line 6124
var termChat = (window.Amenti && Amenti.chat) ? Amenti.chat.create({…}) : null;

And the file it is asking for was loaded eight hundred lines later:

// line 6925 — nine hundred and thirty-six lines TOO LATE.
<script src="amenti-chat.js"></script>

The guard evaluated false. It was written to be safe, and it was: it failed safely, silently, forever.

The guard — (window.Amenti && Amenti.chat) ? … : null — was written to be defensive. And it was perfectly defensive. It failed safely, silently, and permanently, and the inline fallback picked up the conversation without a word.

The handoff said the opposite, in capitals

amenti-chat.js is the PRIMARY PATH — always, normally.
Fixing this file fixes the Terminal AND the Figures in one edit.
That is why it is the highest-leverage file in the system.
— THE HANDOFF, CONFIDENTLY, IN THE PRESENT TENSE

It was not the primary path. It was not any path.

Session after session rebuilt a file the Terminal never loaded — improving an engine that was sitting on the dock, beautifully maintained, connected to nothing.

And fourteen harnesses reported green

This is the part worth carrying forever, because it is a failure of the instrument, not of the code.

Every single harness — 316 assertions of them — did this:

const c = window.Amenti.chat.create({ figure: {…} }); // ← its OWN object
is(typeof c._payload === 'function', 'the anchored window is live'); ✓

The lie

THAT TESTS THE FILE.
IT DOES NOT TEST THE TERMINAL.

The probe stood next to the ship describing the engine, and never asked whether the engine was connected to the propeller.

A test that creates its own object tests the object.
It does not test the system.

The one thing that was never at risk

The money fix held

The anchored window was patched into the inline fallback too — belt and braces, on the theory that "an unguarded fallback is still an open door."

That patch was not a redundancy. It was the only thing running. The 30-exchange break was genuinely fixed and the bleeding genuinely stopped — through the door nobody thought was load-bearing.

The fix, and the tell

Classic script tags execute in document order, synchronously, blocking the parser. So the modules were lifted above the Terminal's IIFE — verified first that none of them touches the DOM at load time, that the throttle must precede the listener, and that the doctrine must precede create(), which is where the engine reads its judgment.

And then the thing that matters more than the fix:

/* THE TELL. A probe can now ask which path the Terminal is on.
The bug hid for the life of the system because NOTHING COULD LOOK AT IT. */
window.Amenti.terminal = termChat; // null = the fallback is live
window.Amenti.terminalPath = termChat ? 'core' : 'inline-fallback';

Amenti.probe now asks. If the Terminal ever drops back to the fallback, the console says so, in red, on the live site.

The recursion — and it is not funny, it is instructive

Making that fix, a comment broke the code twice in ten minutes.

· The explanatory comment written above the fix contained the exact var termChat = … line being searched for. The anchor matched twice. The edit refused.

· The comment written above the moved script tags contained the words "script src" in a sentence — and the verifier read it as a real opening tag, swallowed the four scripts beneath it, and reported the doctrine as "not loaded" when it was sitting right there.

And the worst part

That exact lesson had already been learned that same morning — a "broken script" chased through two wrong hypotheses before anyone simply looked, and found it was the word <script> written inside an HTML comment.

It was written down. It was not installed.

LEARNING A LESSON IS NOT THE SAME AS INSTALLING IT.

Both are installed now: the verifier strips comments before scanning, and the page does not write the tag in prose.

The law this leaves

BEING LOADED IS NOT BEING USED.

A module can be present, correct, versioned, tested by three hundred assertions — and connected to nothing. The manifest can list it. The probe can find its global. The muster can call its name and hear it answer.

None of that is evidence that anybody is calling it.

SECTION VTHE RECOVERY

And it was the captain's probe

The question that found it

The recovery did not come from a test. It came from one question, correctly aimed, at exactly the right moment:

"All these conversation nuances — are they in their own file, or are they spread out? Is there a place for continually adding conversation nuance like the one we just discovered?"
— THE CAPTAIN

There was not. And the probe sent to answer it came back in nine seconds carrying far more than it was sent for:

· the doctrine was spread across four prompt functions, a dozen code constants, two files, and an inline block

· REGISTERS existed twice — and no living person knew it

· the Matrix itself — the actual philosophy — was in a markdown file that nothing reads

A probe returns one hundred times the datum it was sent for.
This one returned the architecture.

And it was asked at the last possible moment

Caught on the gangway

The next thing on the board was the testing phase.

We would have walked into testing with a duplicated register table — two hand-kept copies of a string that composes into /speak, on a system where the style string is part of the cache key.

Edit one, ship it, and the archive begins to re-render. Silently. On a live site. Mid-test. With nobody looking for it.

And the instrument lied while investigating the lie

Worth recording, because it is the doctrine eating itself.

The first comparison run to check whether the two register tables agreed hashed the quote characters — ' versus " — rather than the text, and reported a divergence that did not exist.

An instrument, sent to find a hand-duplication bug, produced a false alarm about it. It had to be caught and re-run properly.

The probe that reports red without looking is worse than no probe. A lie with a light on it.
A probe that corrects itself is worth more than one that is never wrong.

The two captain's probes of this campaign

Neither required reading a line of code. Both outperformed hours of building.

The question What it dragged back 

"Have we considered leaving the microphone on, as a sabotage tool? The microphone is an input source like a keyboard." 
It is not. An unbounded, hands-free, overnight cost loop — the figure hears itself through the speakers, barge fires, its own voice is transcribed [PAID], sent back as the seeker's turn [PAID], and it replies to itself. All night. On your key. Built and unseen. 

"Is there a place for continually adding conversation nuance?" 
No. A duplicated register table on a cache key, and a doctrine scattered across four files — found on the way into testing. 

"Can you check the repo again — I shipped it." 
Six words. The instinct to verify a deploy rather than trust it — and the load order came back wrong, and pulling that thread found the Terminal had never run the conversation core. Not once. Not in its entire life. Without those six words, the next move was "on to the build list." 

Verify, or the silence will lie to you.

Precisely the curl attack — except the attacker is the product.

The standing order

NEVER SEND A HUMAN WHERE A PROBE CAN GO —
BUT THE BEST PROBE IN THE FLEET IS A CAPTAIN
WHO REFUSES TO SAY YES TO EVERYTHING.

"I know that if I just say yes to everything, we will start to take on water."

The fix — amenti-doctrine.js

REGISTERS the six tonal instructions. ONE COPY. Both speakers read it.
MOVES name · expecting · register · gate · AND THE PROMPT LINE THAT TEACHES IT
DETECT arrestHeavy · arrestDismiss · roomAside · roomDecline
DIALS every number the conversation turns on
LAW statements, not questions. Two per question. Kill every "why".
PHASES Threshold → Drawing Out → THE TURN → The Rendering → The Close

The law that made it survivable

DEGRADE SAFELY.
THE DOCTRINE OVERRIDES; IT DOES NOT ENABLE.

Every value has a built-in fallback in the engine. With the doctrine absent, the engine behaves exactly as it did before — 316 assertions prove it. A surface that has not loaded the file is not broken. It is merely plainer.

And the proof that the boundary is now right: the Turn — a brand-new conversational move, with two beats, a register, a gate, and a prompt lesson — went in as DATA. No engine edit. No prompt edit. Add a move with a teach: line and the figure learns it.

SECTION VITHE TURN

The hinge, and the trap

It is two beats, and the gap between them is the instrument

BEAT ONE — the arrest of the whole conversation. Their name, and a full stop:

"Alright, Jason. Let us stop here a moment."

And then nothing. How they meet that pause is itself a reading. Do they say "okay"? Do they brace? Do they rush to fill it?

BEAT TWO — the reflection, OFFERED FOR CORRECTION:

"So, Jason — and correct me if I am wrong — you said a few moments ago that the work was beneath you. And earlier you said your brother would never forgive you. It seems to me those are the same sentence."

The name is the force multiplier

The prompt has always said: hold the name in reserve — use it only where it does real work.

And it never said where that was.

THE TURN IS THAT PLACE.

The one moment in the entire counsel where you say "Jason—" and everything stops. It was a rapport ornament. It is a lever, and it has one correct place to be pulled.

"Correct me if I am wrong" is not manners. It is the mechanism.

It converts the Turn from an assertion into an offer — and it is the offer that makes it safe to anchor on.

This is the near-miss at full scale, with their name on it. People will not volunteer their interior. But they will fix a portrait of themselves that is almost right.

Silence is assent

"…if one is given, continue. And if one is not, continue."
— THE CAPTAIN, SPECIFYING THE GATE

turnhold: { expecting: true, register: 'grave', gate: false }
// ↑
// The figure asks for the floor and TAKES IT EITHER WAY.
// It never strands itself waiting for an "okay" that is not coming.

THE TRAP — and it is the whole design

The trap

THE TURN IS AN OFFER. IT MAY BE WRONG.
SO IT MUST NEVER ANCHOR UNTIL IT IS CONFIRMED.

Anchor an unconfirmed reading and you have permanently installed the figure's misunderstanding at the head of every future payload. It would carry "your brother was right" forever — in a conversation where the seeker already said no, it is that I told everyone I had made it.

That is strictly worse than anchoring on the opening.

Either way, the anchor is something the seeker has SEEN and had the chance to FIX. That is what makes it safe to carry forever.

The convergence — it pays twice

The anchored window kept the opening four messages, on the theory that "the seeker frames themselves at the opening." Raw chatter. Hellos. Throat-clearing.

the opening 4 messages THE TURN 

compact ✗ raw ✓ one paragraph 

high-value ✗ hellos ✓ the distillation of everything 

in the figure's own words ✗ ✓ 

confirmed by the seeker ✗ ✓ they corrected it, or let it stand 

THE COUNSEL PRODUCES IT.
THE COST ARCHITECTURE NEEDS IT.
IT IS THE SAME ARTIFACT.

The rolling summary that was going to "slot in later" never needed building. The Turn IS the rolling summary — and good counsel already demands it.

Verified live

Transcript: 54 messages, whole. Payload: 13 messages — and it now opens with the confirmed Turn, not with "opening chatter number 0."

SECTION VIITHE LAW OF THE MATRIX OF SPEECH

And the standing orders

THE MECHANISM SHOULD BE HARD TO CHANGE.
THE JUDGMENT SHOULD BE TRIVIAL TO CHANGE.

Because the mechanism is engineering — cache keys, state machines, streaming, chunk boundaries — and getting it wrong costs money and breaks trust. It should resist you.

And the judgment is the product. The anchored window is plumbing. "So, Jason — correct me if I am wrong" is what people come for. And it will be wrong, in ways no harness can predict, until real people have talked to it and taught us. It must yield to a ten-second edit.

The word lists are the weakest code in the fleet — and that is the design, not a defect.

The standing orders of speech

Statements, not questions. A question is a demand; a statement is an offer. Correction is the cheapest disclosure there is.

Kill every "why." It demands a justification, so it produces one — rehearsed, defended, useless.

Never be triumphant. Counsel arrives with its price attached, because it did for them. That is the safety mechanism built into the premise.

The Arrest must be rare — and rarity is an OUTCOME, not a policy. A conversation with no arrests in it is a conversation where nothing was hidden.

Sharp in delivery. WARM in intent. Never a gotcha. The figure is not catching them out. It is refusing to let them throw away the true thing.

Acknowledge what announces itself. Never investigate what does not.

Notice, do not record. The data is for the seeker, not about them.

The microphone is not a keyboard. A keyboard is deliberate. A microphone is ambient, and it is untrusted.

The first sentence survives. Always. It is the whole promise.

A counsel that never ends is a subscription.

The figure states what IT cannot do. It never asks what THEY are doing.

Mechanism and judgment are different substances. Consolidate one and you will scatter the other — unless you name the boundary first.

BEING LOADED IS NOT BEING USED. A module can be present, correct, versioned and tested by three hundred assertions — and connected to nothing.

A test that creates its own object tests the object. It does not test the system. The probe stood next to the ship describing the engine, and never asked whether the engine was connected to the propeller.

Learning a lesson is not the same as installing it. A comment ate the code three times in one day, and the lesson had been written down after the first.

Verify, or the silence will lie to you.

And the best probe in the fleet is a captain who refuses to say yes to everything.

The Matrix was a philosophy with no hands.
It has hands now — and one file to hold them.

INGRAM MANOR LLC · THE FLYING DUTCHMAN · NEW YORK HARBOR ◈

Amenti.live — Investment Memorandum

Investment Memorandum · Confidential

AMENTI.AI

A living library of history's greatest minds — conversational AI grounded in verified primary sources.

Stage · Pre-seed
Raising · $500K
June 2026

Prepared for [Investor] — for the named recipient only

&copy; Ingram Manor LLC · All Rights Reserved · 2026

This is an informational memorandum for prospective investors — not a registered securities prospectus or an offer to sell securities. Financial figures are illustrative models built on labeled assumptions, not guarantees. Forward-looking statements involve risk. Any offering will be made only through definitive documents in compliance with applicable securities law; consult counsel before proceeding.

IThe thesis

The Opportunity

Two trends are colliding. Frontier AI has become cheap enough to give anyone a personal, conversational tutor — and education is racing to adopt it, yet recoiling from chatbots that hallucinate and cite nothing.

The Amenti Interface resolves that tension. History has left us a vast treasure trove of content — the words, ideas, and lives of its greatest minds — and almost all of it sits dormant: scattered, intimidating, unread. The Amenti Interface mines that dormant resource and serves it back to people in a way that has never been attempted — awakening those minds and letting anyone hold a real conversation with them — Plato, Confucius, Frederick Douglass — anchored to the verified, public-domain words they actually wrote.

We have already built the hard part: a curated corpus of ~1,100 historical figures, primary-source "reading rooms," original portrait artwork, an in-character chat layer, the Alpha Helix timeline, Personal and Private Counsel, and Treasure Chess — the whole Amenti Interface, live today. We are raising $500K to convert a working product into a multi-channel business — consumer subscriptions, institutional licensing, and offline distribution (in-flight, cruise, museum) — the last of which monetizes our content at near-100% margin with no AI cost at all.

IIWhy it matters

The Problem

· Primary sources are inaccessible. The actual writings of history's great minds are intimidating, scattered, and unread — even as demand for authentic, non-AI-slop content rises.

· AI chat is ungrounded. Consumer "character" chatbots invent quotes and facts; institutions won't adopt tools they can't trust.

· Education wants engagement and rigor. Schools, libraries, and lifelong learners want the pull of conversation with the credibility of cited sources. Nothing on the market cleanly delivers both.

IIIWhat we built

The Product

The Amenti Interface is a library of legends — and a great deal more. Around the catalog of ~1,100 figures it layers distinct ways to explore, converse with, and play against history:

· Browse & discover — ~1,100 figures across 12 taxonomies, each with original portrait artwork and a curated profile card.

· The Alpha Helix — a one-of-a-kind spiral timeline built as a double helix: one strand of sovereigns (the individuals), one of events, coiling along the years so history reads as cyclical rather than linear. Any node opens straight into a figure — or a conversation. Unlike anything else on the web, it is the gateway into the Amenti Interface at large.

· Reading rooms — verified, public-domain primary-source passages (Plato's Republic, Seneca's On the Shortness of Life, the Analects, Douglass's 1845 Narrative), extracted and provenance-checked.

· Personal Counsel — open any figure's dossier and consult that legend one-on-one, grounded in their own documented words and voice.

· Private Counsel — pose a single question to several legends at once. The Counsel of Amenti convenes a panel, each figure answers in their own voice, and the interface returns a synthesized judgment. A boardroom of history, on demand.

StatusBuilt and live. The catalog, taxonomy, reading rooms, figure cards, and a content-automation pipeline are operational. [Insert current metrics for investors: MAU · conversations/mo · retention · waitlist/pilots.]

The Amenti Interface — interactive dialogue, primary-source narrative player, and the mapping framework that binds every response to documented record.

Abraham Lincoln: Personal Counsel. Every figure opens as a living dossier — era, alignment, abilities, disposition, and a "voice print" that governs how they speak — from which you consult that legend one-on-one, anchored to their documented record. One of ~1,100 such files in the Sovereign Roster.

The Alpha Helix: explore cyclical history. The full roster arrayed along a turning spiral of time — every figure set at their year, the centuries coiling inward toward Polaris. History rendered as cyclical rather than linear, and the gateway into the Amenti Interface at large.

The Double Helix: individuals plus events. Two intertwined strands — the Alpha Helix of sovereigns (the people) and the Beta Helix of events — so every figure sits beside what was happening around them: Napoleon against the Louisiana Purchase, the Reign of Terror, Waterloo. History as structure, not a list.

Shaka Zulu: start a conversation. Any node opens the figure's card — temporal anchor, dossier, sources — and a live interface to begin talking with them on the spot. Browsing the timeline and conversing with history are one continuous motion.

Beyond the library sits the game room — Treasure Chess, a three-dimensional naval chess game in which every piece is a historical figure, rendered as a ship under constellation-and-alchemy sails, with a flagship in the Amenti colors. It is real-time and two-player — a lightweight Cloudflare referee relays moves between players — and clicking any vessel opens that figure's biography, so the board and the library draw on a single roster. Several mechanics are its own: a bounty system that puts a price on enemy commanders; an optional Money Mode that tracks each side's winnings; a full checkers variant on the same fleet; and a persistent trophy room where every captured legend is collected — a personal museum of the minds you've taken. Themed scenarios and a records log round it out. It is the engagement surface that turns a reference library into a place people return to.

Treasure Chess. Every piece is a figure from the catalog, rendered as a ship beneath a constellation-and-alchemy sail; the flagship flies the Amenti colors. The roster on the left — Karl Marx, Toussaint Louverture, George Boole, Jacques Cousteau — is the same catalog you browse and converse with elsewhere.

Click any vessel and that figure's biography opens inline — the board and the library are one roster. The menu carries themed Scenarios, a Records log, and the optional Money Mode.

Discovery itself is gamified. Each legend carries a quiz; a clean run posts a streak, unlocks the figure's biography, and mints Star Tokens — a soft in-app currency that opens bonus content and threads the player from the question straight into the reading room and the shop. Alongside it, an AI-reading audio player hosts ~30-minute in-voice sessions — Lincoln's fireside, Caesar at the Rubicon, Hannibal across the Alps — drawn from each figure's own writings. These are rendered once and streamed many times, so they carry the offline economics of the static library, not the live-AI cost.

A recurring editorial spine, Atlantica, publishes a fresh "Today's Dispatch" from the world of the legends — serialized, deep-linked back into the catalog, and built to bring readers back on a schedule. Together with the weekly newsletter, it gives the Amenti Interface a heartbeat of new content without new production cost.

The retention flywheelQuiz → Star Tokens → unlock → reading → conversation → shop, with the Atlantica dispatch and the weekly newsletter pulling players back. Gamified loops and owned-content audio are what turn a reference site into a habit — lowering acquisition cost and lifting free-to-paid conversion, the two numbers that decide whether the unit economics in §VII actually compound.

Small, but powerfulThe entire front end — the full ~1,100-figure catalog, the Alpha Helix, reading rooms, Personal & Private Counsel, Treasure Chess, and commerce — ships in a single file of roughly 1.5 MB. It loads instantly, serves from a static host at near-zero cost, and runs offline. That footprint is not a curiosity; it is precisely what makes the in-flight, cruise, and museum channels — and the near-100%-margin licensing in §VI — physically possible.

IVTiming

Why Now

· Inference is cheap and falling. A grounded conversation now costs ~1–6¢ depending on model tier — a price that did not exist two years ago.

· AI-in-education is at an inflection. Roughly two-thirds of ed-tech vendors are integrating AI; institutions are budgeting for it now.

· The "verified source" wedge is wide open. As hallucination fatigue grows, provenance becomes a premium feature. Our corpus is the moat.

· Falling LEO-satellite connectivity costs are expanding the in-flight and maritime content markets we can license into.

From the source — where the molten record meets the living world

VThe prize

Market Opportunity

Markets Amenti operates across (2026 estimates)
Market 2026 size Growth Relevance 

Global EdTech ~$165–235B ~13–17% CAGR → $375–588B by '33–'35 Core TAM: consumer + institutional

TAM → SAM → SOM. The global EdTech TAM is ~$200B+. Our serviceable market — AI-assisted humanities and primary-source learning, institutional licensing, and curated offline distribution — is a focused slice where our verified-source corpus is differentiated. Initial obtainable market: English-language consumers, early library/school pilots, and one or two IFE/cruise content deals.

VIHow we earn

Business Model

Diversified, stackable revenue streams — consumer to institutional:

· Consumer subscription (freemium). Free tier (ad-supported, capped, lower-cost model) funnels to Pro at $9/mo. The brand and funnel.

· Usage-based / BYO-key. Power users buy metered credits (marked up over cost) or bring their own API key (zero cost to us).

· Institutional licensing — the margin engine. Annual site licenses to libraries, K-12 districts, universities, and museums. Predictable; usage bounded; high margin.

· Offline distribution licensing. The static library needs no network — ideal for in-flight, cruise, hotel, and museum installs, licensed per fleet/venue with no per-use AI cost.

· Advertising & affiliate. Native ads on the free tier plus affiliate links to the full works. Defrays the no-AI-cost browse traffic.

· Commerce — collectibles & books. Limited "Legend Cards" as digital collectibles, plus curated physical books (e.g. a $26 hardcover biography) sold or affiliated from the very page a quiz or reading drops the user onto. Storefront margin on top of near-zero content cost.

· Audio & newsletter. The 30-minute readings seed a free weekly email — one legend, one voice, one half-hour, plus a human contributor essay — that doubles as the cheapest acquisition channel and a sponsorship surface.

On collectibles: the live build issues Legend Cards on-chain. Crypto framing energizes some investors and deters others — the same mechanic works equally as plain digital collectibles or print-on-demand, so emphasis can flex to the room.

The strategic coreThe product has two cost profiles — a zero-marginal-cost static library (now including pre-rendered audio readings and collectibles) and a metered live-AI layer. We monetize the library through high-margin licensing, commerce, and offline deals, and the AI through subscriptions and metering. That separation is the heart of the model.

VIIThe economics

Unit Economics

Current Claude API rates (per million tokens): Haiku 4.5 $1/$5, Sonnet 4.6 $3/$15, Opus 4.8 $5/$25. Prompt caching cuts repeated input 90%; batch is 50% off.

Cost per grounded conversation (~10.6K input + 1.25K output, cached)
Tier Model Cost / conversation 

Free Haiku 4.5 ~$0.012 

Pro Sonnet 4.6 ~$0.035 

Premium Opus 4.8 ~$0.060 

Margin levers we already control: prompt caching (−90% repeated input), model tiering (free users on Haiku), output caps (output is 5× input), batch (−50%), answer-caching for popular figures, and per-user rate limits. Together these cut effective cost 50–80%.

The number that mattersAt cached Sonnet and ~5 conversations/active user/month, AI cost is ~$0.18 per active user per month. Any blended ARPU above ~18¢ is gross-margin positive on inference — and a 3% Pro conversion at $8/mo clears that several times over.

VIIIThe model

Illustrative Financials

Illustrative path — assumptions adjustable (see Appendix)
Stage MAU Consumer rev (yr) AI cost (yr, tiered) Profile 

Launch 50K ~$144K ~$50–70K Near-breakeven; grants fund content 

Growth 100K ~$288K ~$100–130K Consumer margin-positive; licensing = upside 

Scale 1M ~$2.9M ~$0.7–1M Margin expands as licensing dominates mix 

Model tiering keeps blended AI cost well below the all-Sonnet worst case. Each institutional or IFE/cruise license is near-100% margin (capped or offline usage); heavy-user risk is contained by caps and metered overage. The consumer line is the funnel and brand — licensing and distribution are the profit engine.

A live proof point — the Amenti Studios audio archive

The cost discipline this model assumes is not theoretical. The audio-archiving build already running on the stack — full detail in the companion document Amenti Studios — demonstrates the same dynamic at production scale: because rendered content is archived once and replayed free forever, marginal cost is driven by the volume of distinct content produced, not by audience size or traffic. A daily, voiced, AI-narrated dispatch costs on the order of $0.21 per episode to produce, and a full month of daily episodes plus infrastructure runs roughly $12 — a cost floor that barely moves as the audience scales.

Adapted from Amenti Studios, &sect;Cost projections. Production cost falls from roughly 43% of revenue at the Seedling stage to about 1% at Established — the same asymmetry the consumer/AI model above depends on, already observed in a shipping feature.

Moat: a proprietary, provenance-checked corpus and original artwork that took real curatorial effort; the verified-source trust position; institutional contracts once seeded; and an offline-licensable asset that chat-only competitors cannot easily replicate.

Ownership & IPIngram Manor LLC is the legal entity that owns and controls the website and the Amenti Interface, together with all associated intellectual property and digital property and assets — source code, the curated corpus, original artwork, brand, and data — including the domains Amenti.AI and Amenti.live. All assets described in this memorandum are held within this single entity.

XThe plan

Go-to-Market & the Ask

Phased roadmap

· Phase 0 — Harden economics. Auth, caps, caching, tiering, metering. Protects margin from day one.

· Phase 1 — Consumer launch (0–50K). Freemium + ads/affiliate; apply for non-dilutive grants (NEH, Mellon, IMLS, Knight).

· Phase 2 — Institutional (50–100K). Library/school/university pilots → paid licenses; metered credits for power users.

· Phase 3 — Distribution & scale (100K–1M). In-flight/cruise/museum licensing as recurring backbone; enterprise/white-label; international.

The ask & use of funds

Raising $500K on a post-money SAFE to reach metering and distribution live, the first three to five institutional / in-flight licenses, and meaningful consumer traction — proof the licensing backbone converts. With no founder salary and no planned hires through this milestone, burn is concentrated in infrastructure, content, and partnerships, so $500K funds roughly 18 months of runway.

XIIIThe long game

Vision & Exit

The Amenti Interface aims to become the definitive living library — the trusted place where anyone, anywhere, can converse with history grounded in truth.

This product is singularly unique. Nothing else has attempted what it does: awakening the greatest minds in history and deploying them across use cases that are at once educational, entertaining, and utilitarian — a tutor, a companion, a counsel, a game. History's treasure is already paid for and out of copyright; the Amenti Interface is the mechanism that brings it back to life and puts it to work.

The long-term asset is a provenance-checked corpus, a trusted brand, recurring institutional revenue, and a distribution footprint across screens, classrooms, airplanes, and ships. Strategic acquirers and partners — ed-tech platforms, educational publishers, digital-library providers, and in-flight/cruise content aggregators — all value curated content, recurring B2B revenue, and a defensible verified-source position.

§Plain terms

Glossary

API callA single request to the language model. Every chat message is one or more API calls, billed by the tokens it uses.
TokenThe unit of text the model is billed on — roughly ¾ of a word, or about 4 characters.
Prompt cachingReusing an identical block of input (e.g., a figure's character prompt) so it is billed at ~10% of the normal input price on repeat.
Batch APISubmitting non-urgent work to be processed within ~24 hours at a 50% discount.
Model tieringRouting cheaper requests to a low-cost model (Haiku) and premium ones to a stronger model (Sonnet/Opus) to control cost.
MAUMonthly Active Users — people who use the product in a given month.
ARPUAverage Revenue Per User — total revenue divided by users; here, the bar each user must clear to be profitable.
FreemiumA free tier that funnels a small share of users to a paid subscription.
Star TokensA soft in-app currency earned through quizzes and streaks; spent to unlock biographies, readings, and bonus content. Drives retention, not revenue directly.
Legend CardsLimited digital collectibles tied to a figure, which can unlock bonus readings or audio commentary. Issued on-chain in the current build; equally workable as plain digital collectibles.
Conversion rateThe share of free users who upgrade to paid (typically 2–5% for content apps).
BYO-key"Bring Your Own Key" — the user supplies their own API key, so their usage bills to them, not to us.
Pass-through / metered billingCharging users for their usage (via prepaid credits) and paying the provider from the pool, keeping a markup.
Gross marginRevenue minus the direct cost of delivering it (here, mostly inference) — the profitability of each sale.
TAM / SAM / SOMTotal / Serviceable / Obtainable Market — the full market, the slice we can serve, and the slice we can realistically win.
IFE / IFECIn-Flight Entertainment (and Connectivity) — onboard content systems, often offline, that we can license into.
SSOSingle Sign-On — institutional login (e.g., a library card or university account) used to authenticate licensed access.
SAFESimple Agreement for Future Equity — a common early-stage investment instrument that converts to shares later.
Non-dilutive fundingCapital (e.g., grants) that does not require giving up ownership.

AThe detail

Appendix

A · Per-conversation token model

A typical session: a ~1,500-token persona prompt, 5 user turns (~40 tokens each), 5 assistant replies (~250 tokens each), with history resent each turn → ~10,600 input + 1,250 output tokens.

Model No caching With caching 

Haiku 4.5 ~$0.017 ~$0.012 

Sonnet 4.6 ~$0.051 ~$0.035 

Opus 4.8 ~$0.084 ~$0.060 

B · Monthly AI cost at scale

Assumption: 5 conversations / active user / month. Browse-only traffic is ~$0.

MAU Conv / mo Haiku Sonnet Opus 

50K 250K ~$3.0K ~$8.8K ~$15K 

100K 500K ~$6.0K ~$17.5K ~$30K 

1M 5M ~$60K ~$175K ~$300K 

C · Sensitivity

Cost scales linearly with engagement: light (2 conv/user/mo) ≈ ×0.4; heavy (15 conv/user/mo) ≈ ×3. The average user is safe; the uncapped heavy user and the anonymous bot are the risk — controlled by caps and auth, not revenue.

D · Cost-control levers

· Prompt caching — −90% on repeated input (the persona prompt + injected sources).

· Model tiering — free users on Haiku (~⅓ the cost of Sonnet).

· Output caps — output is 5× input; a concise mode lowers cost materially.

· Answer-caching — serve pre-generated responses to common questions with no live call.

· Batch API — −50% for non-interactive generation (dispatches, FAQs).

E · Technical architecture

Requests flow through a Cloudflare Worker proxy, which is the natural enforcement point for per-user metering, quotas, model-tier routing, and prompt-cache headers. Static library content (reading rooms, cards, portraits) is served directly and carries no inference cost — the basis of the offline-licensable module. See the product diagram in §III.

All market figures are third-party estimates and should be independently source-cited before distribution. This memorandum does not constitute investment, legal, or tax advice.

AS ABOVE, SO BELOW · Amenti
· 

Architecture Note·The Shelf·Ingram Manor LLC

23 July 2026·2026-07-23·Rev. B

Amenti.live · What Rests On What

As Above,SO BELOW

Five Strata · One Floor · Two Directions

At the end of the ledger dedupe the captain asked whether the spreadsheet is the ground floor — nothing beneath it but the dead who are in the ground. It is, for one thing, and it is not, for another. That single distinction decides where every future repair belongs, and it is the whole of this document.

REV. B. Rev. A was written mid-diagnosis, while the fault was still running. It has since been repaired &#8212; see &#167;6. The counts here are the deployed ones, the side door turned out smaller than it looked, and codexFor is now past tense. &#167;4 is unchanged: a measurement of a real failure is worth more than a tidied one.

THE LEDGER DECIDES WHO EXISTS.
IT DOES NOT DECIDE WHAT IS TRUE.

The five strata

Nothing here is new construction. It is what the fleet already is, drawn as a section so the load paths are visible. Read it from the bottom, the way a building is built.

Figure 1 · the section. Gold is the floor; hatching is bedrock.

# Stratum What it settles Where it lives Count 

V The Seeker what a living person makes of a dead one mint · progress · docket &#8212; 

IV The Quiz which moment of the life is asked supabase topics 59 

III The Character face, sheet, words &#8212; who they were, in house terms Page1 + characters 109 

II The Ledger who exists at all the Sheet 1,002 

I The Dead what happened nowhere &#8212; reached by Links and search &#8734; 

Stratum III stood at 38 when Rev. A was written. It is 109 now &#8212; not because 71 people were added, but because 71 already on the roll call as a name and a rank finally received a sheet. The floor did not grow; the storey above it filled in.

Why the floor is identity and not truth

A ledger row asserts a person. It does not assert a fact. THE QUIZZARD already says this outright, and it is the reason the engine was built the way it was:

&#8220;A library folder is NOT required. Lincoln has a full reading room; Leif has only this row. Both produce equally good quizzes, because the substance comes from verified research, not from pre-existing depth. The row is the anchor; history is the source.&#8221;

So the engine does not read history out of the spreadsheet. It reads a name, then goes past the floor with the search tool and verifies against the record itself. That is what the Links column is for, and it is why a model writing from memory is the single failure the whole doctrine exists to prevent.

Two loads, two paths. Identity rises from the floor and everything above inherits it. Verification ignores the floor entirely and goes to bedrock. Confuse them and you get a system that is confident about people who never existed.

AN ERROR AT THE FLOOR IS INVISIBLE FROM EVERY STOREY ABOVE IT.
NOTHING UPSTAIRS CAN DETECT WHAT THE GROUND GOT WRONG.

The proof, taken this week

This is not a metaphor about architecture. It was measured on the running page, on the night of 22&#8211;23 July, before any repair. One fault at the floor &#8212; the same person entered twice &#8212; amplified as it climbed:

Stratum What the fault became there Measured 

II · ledger one person, two rows, two ranks 94 people 

III · character aliases claimed by two records 300 

III · character people resolving to the wrong human by surname 191 

III · character &#8230;of those, drawing another person&#8217;s face and bars 4 

IV · quiz quiz figures joining a character by exact key 0 of 53 

Ninety-four duplicated rows became three hundred collisions two storeys up. The matcher that hid it &#8212; last name, then containment &#8212; was itself a repair attempted at the wrong altitude: written at stratum III to survive a fault at stratum II. It worked, which is the dangerous part. It matched a pope to Cleopatra on a regnal numeral and seven monarchs to Constantine on the word great, and the arena looked perfectly well the whole time.

A FIX APPLIED ABOVE THE FAULT IS NOT A FIX.
IT IS A DISGUISE, AND IT WILL BE MAINTAINED FOR YEARS.

The side door

One correction to the clean model, and it is factual rather than philosophical. The floor has a door in the side of it. Six curated figures were admitted straight into stratum III and appear in no ledger row at all:

musashi hannibal cleopatra
tacitus manlius frederick-douglass

This is not a defect. The curated set is a supplement by design, and mergeCuratedOver() exists precisely to layer it over the roll call. But it means the sentence &#8220;the ledger decides who exists&#8221; is true of the thousand and false of the thirty-eight &#8212; and any component that assumes a single point of admission will be wrong about eight people.

Rev. A said eight, and named Gibbon and Stoker among them. That count came from an older seven-column export of the ledger; both are present in the live twelve-column one. The lesson is the document&#8217;s own: a count taken from the wrong copy of the floor is wrong on every storey that quotes it.

THE FLOOR HAS A SIDE DOOR.
THE RESOLVER MUST ANSWER FOR BOTH ENTRANCES, OR IT IS NOT A RESOLVER.

What was done &#183; 23 July 2026

The fault in &#167;4 is closed. Recorded here so the diagnosis above reads as history rather than as a standing condition.

Rev. A Rev. B 

duplicated ledger people 94 0 

name-matching schemes 6 1 

wrong by surname 191 of 319 0 

quiz figures joining by key 0 of 53 53 of 53 

alias collisions 300 0 

rich characters 38 109 

canonical keys renamed &#8212; none 

The repair obeyed the law above: it was made at the stratum that owned the fault. The ledger was deduped in the ledger &#8212; by field-level merge, so twenty-five better biographies were carried onto surviving rows rather than deleted, and two different men who shared the name Francis Bacon were split rather than collapsed. Only then was the lookup replaced.

codexFor is retired. In its place, exact match or nothing: four written forms of a name are indexed, and an unrecognised name returns unknown rather than a plausible neighbour. Seventeen adversarial pairs &#8212; each a real wrong answer taken from the live page &#8212; are asserted on every load.

Nothing was renamed, so art keys, topic ids, reading rooms, group maps, bookmarked URLs and every recorded seeker progress row kept working untouched. The full account is in VELVET RESOLVER.

THE FLOOR WAS REPAIRED FROM THE FLOOR.
NOT ONE STOREY ABOVE IT WAS ASKED TO COMPENSATE.

What only the top floor does

Worth naming, because it is the reason the rest is built. Strata I through IV are retrieval and rendering &#8212; a dead person, recorded, dressed, and asked about. Nothing there creates anything that was not already in the world.

Stratum V does. When a seeker files a case to the docket and argues a legacy, a living human adds a thing that did not exist before &#8212; the only genuinely new matter in the whole section. Everything beneath it is scaffolding for that one act.

Which is also the correct priority ordering when time is short: a fault at the floor threatens the building; a fault at the top threatens the point of it.

The rules that fall out

· Repair at the stratum that owns the fault. If two rows describe one person, that is a ledger repair. Do not teach the matcher to cope.

· No storey may invent a person. A character, a quiz or a card may only name someone admitted at stratum II or through the side door. Anything else is a figure with no ground under it.

· Identity is assigned once, at the floor, and never re-derived. Six private derivations produced six answers. A key is stored, not computed &#8212; and an alias table is how the other five spellings are forgiven without being trusted.

· Verification skips the floor. The engine goes to bedrock with search. A row is an anchor and never a citation.

· Every stratum fails visibly. The glass gate applies at each level: show what was read or show nothing. A layer that quietly substitutes a plausible answer is worse than one that stops &#8212; and a fallback that swaps a thousand figures for thirty-eight without a word is only half a gate.

· Count people, not rows. The codex advertised 1,108 for months. There were about a thousand. A number nobody computes is decoration.

· Read the floor you are actually standing on. Rev. A miscounted the side door from a stale export. Every storey that quotes a floor count inherits its error.

Done when

One name resolves to one person through one function, at every stratum &#8212; and asking the ship &#8220;how many legends are there&#8221; returns a number it actually counted.

Status

Held at the arena, the cards and the engine&#8217;s collision test. Three consumers remain on their own schemes: library.js, mergeCuratedOver, and the roster&#8217;s card grouping.

AS ABOVE, SO BELOW.
WHAT IS TRUE OF ONE ROW MUST BE TRUE OF THE FACE, THE QUIZ,
AND THE JUDGEMENT THAT RESTS ON IT.

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · AS ABOVE, SO BELOW · architecture note · 2026-07-23 · Rev. B
