THE CARD BAY · Amenti
· 

Specification&#183;The Shelf&#183;Ingram Manor LLC

20 July 2026 &#183; 11:20 UTC&#183;Rev. A

Amenti.live &#183; Where a Stack Lives

TheCARD BAY

One Figure &#183; One Card &#183; Whatever the Depth

Gate rows were right when a figure held two quizzes and stop working at five. A card that grows with its stack breaks the grid it sits in, and the grid is what makes the arena scannable. So the stack moves off the card and into a bay behind it.

What rows do at seven gates, and what the bay does instead.

THE CARD STAYS A CARD.
ONE FIGURE, ONE TILE, UNIFORM HEIGHT, WHATEVER THE DEPTH.

The problem, stated exactly

A gate row is about forty pixels. Two rows sit comfortably beneath a portrait and stat bars. Seven rows is two hundred and eighty pixels of list on a tile that was two hundred tall to begin with &#8212; and the arena becomes a column of cards at wildly different heights, which is the one thing a grid cannot survive.

This is not hypothetical. Caesar already holds two and the engine is ready for depth 2 and 3 across the roster. The first time the patrol runs at depth, several figures reach five.

The bay

Surface What it holds Behaviour 

the arena one card per figure, the face, the era, the stat bars, and a gate count uniform height, always 

the bay the open card large, and a strip of the rest beneath it opens on CLICK 

Click, not hover

Hover reveals fast and is hostile on touch, and this is a deliberate act rather than a preview. The card carries the count &#8212; &#8220;7 gates&#8221; &#8212; so a seeker knows a bay exists before committing to open it.

All at once, not paged

A seeker opening Jefferson wants to know what is there before spending ninety seconds. Four moments visible is orientation. Four moments one at a time is a corridor.

The strip is the argument

This is the part that matters beyond layout. Jefferson&#8217;s bay reads:

the Declaration &#160;&#183;&#160; Monticello &#160;&#183;&#160; the Louisiana Purchase &#160;&#183;&#160; Sally Hemings

That fourth title tells a seeker what kind of place this is before they answer a single question. A library that holds Jefferson and does not hold Hemings has made a choice; so has one that does. You cannot say that by paging &#8212; they would have to earn their way to it, and by then it is a reveal rather than a promise.

The same is true of Columbus: navigation and the Ta&#237;no, side by side, visible from the first look.

Locked cards show their titles

Dimmed, unclickable, and legible. Hiding a locked title would be the platform flinching at its own premise. Knowing that Jefferson&#8217;s fourth gate is Sally Hemings is exactly the thing worth earning your way to.

A PLACE BUILT TO WEIGH THE DEAD CANNOT BE COY ABOUT WHAT IT IS WEIGHING.

What this replaces, and what it inherits

Gate rows are superseded. They were correct for two and are the wrong shape for five. The bay takes their place.

The label work carries straight over. The short names written an hour before this decision &#8212; the Rubicon, the last words, the swan-neck flask &#8212; are exactly what a strip needs. That was built for rows and turns out to have been built for this.

And it gives the scenes somewhere to live. Each card in the bay carries its own moment&#8217;s picture, which is a thing a forty-pixel row was never going to do. Caesar at the river on one, the Senate floor on another.

Three decisions in one evening have now converged on the same dependency: the roster wants a style, the calendar dispatch wants the right picture of the right moment, and the bay wants a picture per card. All three are waiting on scenes. That is the strongest argument yet for building them next.

Still open

Not decided, and not urgent

the lock every gate currently waits for the one above. Defensible &#8212; but so is showing them all and letting depth be a suggestion rather than a wall. A seeker stuck on one question is stuck entirely. 

the strip at twelve Caesar could hold a dozen. A strip scrolls; a grid wraps. Whichever it is, the count on the card must be honest about the number. 

where the bay lives an overlay over the arena, or its own address. An address is shareable and bookmarkable, which matters more than it sounds. 

GATE ROWS WERE RIGHT FOR TWO.
THE BAY IS RIGHT FOR AS MANY AS A LIFE HOLDS.

Amenti.live &#183; Ingram Manor LLC &#183; THE CARD BAY &#183; specification &#183; 2026-07-20 &#183; Rev. A

THE EQUILIBRIUM · Amenti
· 

Engineering Brief&#183;The Shelf&#183;Ingram Manor LLC

20 July 2026 &#183; 13:40 UTC&#183;Rev. A

Amenti.live &#183; The Emerald Economy, Read Rather Than Assumed

TheEQUILIBRIUM

One Hundred Endorsements A Week

Two days added three ways for Emerald Tokens to enter the world and none for them to leave it, which looked like a problem. Four queries later it is not one &#8212; the sink is real, the arithmetic balances at a number, and nobody had noticed because the whole economy has never once been exercised.

512
Minted
three faucets

0
Spent
ever

0
Endorsements
the only sink

100
Break-Even
votes per week

What was assumed, and what the ledger said

The assumption &#8212; mine, stated twice in earlier documents &#8212; was that the endorsement at 5 ET might be a transfer into the weekly pool rather than a burn, and that the pool might be minting out of nothing. Both would have meant an economy that only ever grows.

Neither survived contact with the functions.

Every faucet, the single sink, and the number where they cancel.

The sink is real, and it is careful

insert into emerald_ledger (user_id, amount, reason, reference)
values (p_voter, -v_cost, 'vote_spend', 'vote:' || p_argument);

A genuine burn. A negative row, append-only, tokens destroyed rather than moved. And cast_argument_vote is guarded more thoroughly than most of what surrounds it: the argument must be visible, no self-endorsement, no double-endorsement, a balance check before the debit, and a race handled through unique_violation rather than hoped away.

And it cannot be reached alone

Two of those guards combine into something nobody designed but everybody should notice.

rule 2 you cannot endorse your own argument 

rule 3 you must have written a visible argument to earn the right to endorse at all 

In a hall with one person, those are mutually exclusive. You write to earn the right, and then the only arguments available are yours, which is blocked. Not a fault: endorsement is a social act and it is correct that it does not work alone. But it explains the zero, and it means the economy has been untestable rather than untested.

THE SINK IS NOT TOO SMALL AND IT IS NOT BROKEN.
IT IS UNREACHABLE UNTIL THERE ARE TWO PEOPLE IN THE HALL.

The pool mints &#8212; and it is still coherent

settle_argument_pool writes pool_reward as a POSITIVE row, distributed by vote share:

floor(p_pool * v.votes / tot.t) &#8212; from a fixed POOL_SIZE, not from what was burned

So the pool is a fourth faucet. That is the thing I expected to be a problem, and it is not, because the burn scales with participation while the mint does not.

Endorsements in a week Burned Minted Net supply 

50 250 500 +250 

100 500 500 0 

200 1,000 500 &#8722;500 

500 2,500 500 &#8722;2,000 

EQUILIBRIUM = POOL_SIZE / VOTE_COST = 500 / 5 = ONE HUNDRED ENDORSEMENTS A WEEK.
BELOW IT THE SUPPLY GROWS. ABOVE IT, A BUSIER HALL DESTROYS MORE THAN IT PAYS.

That is the right direction, and it is the opposite of what most token systems do. Activity here is deflationary: the more the court argues, the scarcer the token becomes, and the pool a winner takes is worth more precisely because more was burned to award it.

Whether that was designed or fell out of two constants chosen months apart on separate afternoons, it is coherent &#8212; and it means there is no inflation problem to fix. There is a threshold to watch.

The faucets, and what two days added

Reason What it pays for Run so far 

quiz_pass a question answered correctly, once ever, per question 390 

readaloud a passage heard 72 

quiz_mastery every question right in one sitting &#8212; 50 50 

quiz_complete all six marks held, however long it took &#8212; 20 never run 

pool_reward the weekly 500, by vote share never run 

Every one is protected by the same unique index: (user_id, reason, reference). A question cannot pay twice, a bonus cannot pay twice, and a settlement cannot pay a week twice. That is enforced by the database, not by a Worker, which is why no guard was written for the retry when the Gates were built &#8212; none was needed.

What this changes about the Exchange

Very little, and that is the useful finding.

The relic shop was framed in earlier documents as urgent &#8212; the only thing standing between the library and a number that means nothing. It is not urgent. The economy already balances at a hundred endorsements a week, and the shop becomes a second sink rather than the first one.

What it would add is choice. Endorsement is the only thing to spend on, and it is social by nature: you cannot spend alone, you cannot spend on yourself, and you cannot spend twice on the same argument. A relic is a private act, and a private sink is what lets a solitary seeker participate in the economy at all.

What to watch, rather than what to build

the threshold a hundred endorsements a week is break-even. Well below it and the supply grows; the fix is a smaller pool, not a bigger burn. 

the first settle settle_argument_pool has never run. The first one is worth watching by hand, exactly as the first patrol was. 

the second person the sink is unreachable until the hall has two. Everything about the economy is theoretical until then &#8212; including this document. 

FOUR QUERIES SETTLED WHAT TWO DESIGN DOCUMENTS ASSUMED.
READ THE FUNCTION BEFORE PLANNING AROUND WHAT IT PROBABLY DOES.

Amenti.live &#183; Ingram Manor LLC &#183; THE EQUILIBRIUM &#183; 2026-07-20 &#183; Rev. A

  BRIEF 21 OF 50  ·  THE GATES · AMENTI

==============================================================================

THE GATES · Amenti
· 

Specification·The Shelf·Ingram Manor LLC

19 July 2026·2026-07-19·Rev. B

Amenti.live · The Progression System

TheGATES

Six Hearts · Every Question Pays Once · The Gate Opens

In the Book of the Dead the weighing is not the end. Afterwards the dead pass through a series of gates, and each opens only to one who knows the name. Not judgement — knowledge, tested at a threshold. That is what this is.

EVERY QUESTION PAYS ONCE — WHENEVER YOU FIRST GET IT RIGHT.
THE WEIGHING HAPPENS ONCE. THE GATE MAY BE RETURNED TO UNTIL IT OPENS.

What this changes

Today a quiz is a test: one attempt, a score, an end. The proposal is that it becomes a lock. Six hearts, and the next set of questions on that figure opens. A seeker does not pass or fail — they either know it yet or they do not.

The move that makes it work is separating two things that were tangled together. The weighing is once. The questions are practice until mastered.

Figure 1 · the gate, and the line between what happens once and what may be returned to.

Happens ONCE May be RETURNED TO 

what the two cases, filed to the docket · the tally · the verdict any question not yet held — and it pays when you get it right, whenever that is 

why you do not re-weigh a heart. The cases are already argued and endorsed; re-filing them would corrupt the docket. knowing a thing later is still knowing it, and the work is the same work. A gate that never opens is not a gate, it is a wall. 

The payout, revised

An earlier draft held that the first attempt set the tier and a retry paid nothing — progress without money. That was a rule invented to prevent a farm.

The rule is unnecessary, because the ledger already prevents it. Every correct answer writes quiz:topic:qid — a reference that is per question, not per attempt. A question can therefore only ever pay once, whenever it is first answered correctly. A retry pays only for hearts not yet held. There is nothing to enforce; the reference enforces it.

So the simpler design is also the safer one: each question pays when you get it right. Go back a week later, answer the one you missed, and you are paid for that one. The four you already hold pay nothing, because they already did.

ONE THING MUST HOLD FOR THIS TO STAY SAFE:
A WRONG ANSWER MUST NOT REVEAL THE CORRECT ONE.

If it did, a seeker could answer deliberately wrong to scout the answer, then retry and take the full timed tier for speed. Without disclosure a retry is exactly what it appears to be — I went away and learned it. That is the whole guard, and it costs nothing to keep.

The two bonuses

Two separate achievements, and they should not be confused. Each needs its own reference so each pays once.

Reference Earned by What it rewards 

quiz:{topic}:complete all six hearts held, however long it took persistence — you came back until you knew it 

quiz:{topic}:flawless all six in a single sitting mastery — you knew it walking in 

The existing MASTERY_BONUS = 50 becomes the second. The completion bonus should sit meaningfully below it: persistence is real and worth paying for, but it is not the same achievement, and the gap between the two numbers is what makes the flawless run worth attempting.

Both are references, not flags — so a seeker who eventually completes a quiz gets the completion bonus once, and a seeker who sweeps it first time gets both: the flawless bonus AND the completion, since completing it is what they did. That is correct. The flawless run is strictly better, and the arithmetic says so without a special case.

The hearts already exist

Every correct answer already writes a row: reason: 'quiz_pass', reference: 'quiz:manlius-capitol:geese'. That reference is unique per seeker, per question. Six correct answers are six rows.

So which hearts a seeker holds is already recorded. Nothing new is stored. What is missing is one endpoint that reads it back.

Figure 2 · the data is there. One read, and the query that must precede all of it.

RUN THE QUERY BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE.
IF RETAKES ALREADY PAY TWICE, THAT IS A LEAK IN THE LEDGER TODAY — WITH OR WITHOUT GATES.

The build

Gate IKnow What Is Already Truefirst

One query, before a line is written.

select count(*) as rows,
count(distinct reference) as refs
from emerald_ledger
where reason = 'quiz_pass';

Equal — the RPC refuses duplicates. A retry cannot pay again, and the whole design is safe by construction.

Unequal — the same question has already paid more than once. That is a leak that exists today, independent of this spec, and it is fixed before anything is built on top of it.

Done when
You know which of those two sentences is true.

Gate IIThe Progress Endpointone read

GET /quiz/progress on the mint, authenticated as the seeker. It reads their quiz_pass references, groups by topic, and returns which question ids are held.

{ "manlius-capitol": { "held": ["geese","surname","rock"], "of": 6, "mastered": false },
"caesar-rubicon": { "held": ["river","legion","why-war","die-cast"], "of": 4, "mastered": true } }

Note what it does not return: no answers, no keys, no correctness for questions not yet held. A question the seeker has not got right is simply absent — which is the same information a hollow heart carries, and nothing more.

Done when
A seeker's own hearts can be read back, and a stranger's cannot.

Gate IIIThe Retrypays what is not yet held

Re-entering a quiz already taken offers only the questions not yet held. The passage is there to read. The two cases are not asked again — they are already on the docket.

does open the heart, and pay for that question, at the tier earned on this attempt 

does pay quiz:{topic}:complete when the sixth heart lands 

does NOT pay again for a heart already held — the reference refuses it 

does NOT pay flawless, which only a single unbroken sitting earns 

does NOT re-file the cases, re-run the tally, or alter the verdict 

Note what does not need building: no attempt counter, no "first pass" flag, no per-topic state table. The ledger already carries every fact this needs, because the reference was written per question rather than per session. That decision was made long before anyone thought about gates, and it is the reason this is a small build.

Done when
A retry pays for a heart newly opened and cannot move the balance for one already held.

Gate IVThe Card Shows Itthe point of all of it

Six hearts on the card — filled for held, hollow for not. The score beside each. And beneath, the state of the next gate.

This is what makes the arena a collection rather than a menu: a seeker scanning the roster sees at a glance where they are incomplete, and the hollow heart is an invitation rather than a scold.

Decide before building

· What does a signed-out visitor see? If hearts appear only after sign-in, the arena looks emptier to a stranger than to you — and the stranger is the one being persuaded. A greyed set with "sign in to begin" is likely better than nothing.

· Is the next set gated or signposted? "Six hearts to open the next" is a real unlock and a real risk: a seeker stuck on one question is stuck entirely. Signposting is softer and cannot trap anyone.

· Does the sixth heart look different? The moment a gate opens is the strongest beat in the system. It should not look like the fifth.

Done when
A returning seeker can see, without opening anything, which gates they have passed and which are one answer from opening.

What this supersedes

THE WEIGHING, Movement IV — "the punched card" — is struck. It proposed three hearts meaning three quizzes completed. This is six hearts meaning six questions mastered: a different unit, a smaller grain, and a stronger idea. The Weighing keeps Movements I to III, which are about what a seeker sees. This document governs what a seeker can do.

The order

# Move Touches 

1 Run the duplicate-reference query nothing 

2 GET /quiz/progress the mint · read only 

3 Hearts on the card the client 

4 The retry · the two bonuses · no disclosure on a wrong answer the mint · writes 

5 The next gate, once the multi-quiz stack exists the engine and the card 

A note on the other side of the ledger

This adds faucets: every question pays, and two bonuses sit on top. The spending side already exists in part — VOTE_COST = 5 per endorsement is a genuine spend, live today, and it is the only one.

Two things are worth establishing before the shop is designed, and neither blocks this build:

1 Is the endorsement a burn or a transfer? If the 5 ET funds the weekly pool it is redistributed and the supply never falls. If it vanishes, the economy genuinely contracts somewhere. 

2 Where does POOL_SIZE = 500 come from? Minted fresh each week makes the pool a faucet of its own — which may be correct, but should be a decision rather than an accident. 

The relic shop, trade fees and endorsement burns were all designed in THE EXCHANGE and none are built. Rewards first is the right order — you cannot price a shop before you know what people hold. But the answers to those two questions decide what the shop has to do, so they are worth knowing early.

A GATE THAT NEVER OPENS IS NOT A GATE — IT IS A WALL.
A GATE THAT OPENS FOR ANYONE IS NOT A GATE EITHER.

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · THE GATES · specification · 2026-07-19 · Rev. B

THE LEIF BRIEF · Amenti
· 

Engineering Brief·The Shelf·Ingram Manor LLC

19 July 2026 · 09:40 UTC·2026-07-19·Rev. A

Amenti.live · Engineering Brief · XIII

The Leif
Brief

The quiet prize

Ingram Manor LLC · one row in a manifest of eleven hundred

The crossing · and the magazine behind it

One row in a manifest of eleven hundred. No reading room, no portrait, no quiz.

This is the account of what was built between that row and this morning.

He was one row in a spreadsheet. Rank five hundred and one, Explorer, no reading room, no portrait, no quiz — a name on a manifest of eleven hundred names, most of which nobody had ever opened. The library could not have told you he was there. This is the account of what was built between that row and this morning, when a machine with no one watching wrote three quizzes about gods and filed them for review.

I · One row

The curated twenty-one had rooms. Caesar had eight hundred kilobytes of his own Commentarii; Tesla had his London lectures; Aurelius had the Meditations entire. Around them the fleet had built voices, portraits, codex records, stat lines — a deep and careful architecture for figures the captain had chosen by hand.

Leif Erikson had none of it. He sat in names.csv with a rank and a one-word title, in a column of eight hundred and seventy-five other dead men and women who had exactly the same.

The question was not whether a quiz could be written about him. Anyone can write a quiz. The question was whether one could be written without the room — whether the depth that made Caesar workable was a requirement or a luxury. Because if it was a requirement, the library was twenty-one figures deep and always would be. And if it was not, it was eleven hundred.

THE PROOF WAS NOT THE QUIZ.
THE PROOF WAS THAT NOTHING HAD TO BE BUILT FIRST.

The Vinland quiz was written from public sources in an afternoon: the westward crossing around the year 1000, the timber and salmon and wild grapes he named the place for, the sagas disagreeing over whether he sought it or was blown off course, and the settlement at L'Anse aux Meadows unearthed in 1960 that turned five centuries of dismissed legend into fact.

It stood beside the hand-built ones without apology. And it proved the thing the whole roster depended on: a figure needs a verifiable moment and a real argument. It does not need a room.

II · The voyage

Figure 1 · from one row to a patrol that chooses its own subjects.

What followed was not planned as a campaign. Each piece was built because the piece before it had made the next one obvious.

If a quiz could be written for any figure, the bottleneck was no longer knowledge — it was the hand that wrote it. Every quiz in the library had come out of a conversation, and a conversation does not scale to eight hundred and seventy-five.

So the library left the Worker that moves money and became a table, because you cannot generate into a constant. So an engine was built that takes one name and returns a verified quiz or an honest refusal. So the engine was armed with search, because a model writing from memory is precisely the fabrication the doctrine exists to prevent. So a validator was written that is a parser rather than a review, and a normaliser that supplies constants and never content.

Then the modes, each because a band of the roster demanded it. LIFE for the eight hundred who have a record. CANON for the hundred and fifty who have only a tradition — Apollo, Enki, the scriptures — where the claim is about a surviving text and never about a life. SOURCE for the twenty-one with rooms, where the passage is not written at all but quoted, and the Worker string-matches it against the file so that fabrication is not discouraged but impossible.

Then the stack, because a figure is not worth one quiz. The Rubicon alone is three: what happened, why it was treason, and where the river actually is — which is still argued between three candidates, alongside what he said crossing it, which Suetonius and Plutarch do not agree on.

And then the patrol, because a machine that waits for a button is still a hand that writes.

III · Where it stands this morning

46
Quizzes Live
from 13

~20
Machine-Written
unattended

786
In the Magazine
749 pending

$0.11
Per Round
measured twice

At three figures an hour the mythic tier finishes inside two days and the whole queue in ten. The engine chooses the moment. It chose the slaying of Python for Apollo, the reassembly of Osiris for Isis, and for Enki the reed wall — the god sworn not to warn humanity of the flood, who spoke to a wall and let a man overhear, keeping the letter of an oath while breaking all of it.

Nobody suggested that. It went and found it.

IV · The quiet prize

The forty-six quizzes are the visible result. They are not the valuable one.

Every figure the engine refuses is written down with the reason it refused: contested-existence for Homer, whose life cannot be asserted; empty-pan for a figure with nothing honest to argue against them; no-reading-room, no-further-pivot, failed-validation. One line per refusal, accumulating quietly beneath the work.

AFTER A COUPLE OF HUNDRED ROWS THAT LOG STOPS BEING A RECORD OF FAILURES
AND BECOMES A DESCRIPTION OF THE ROSTER — EVIDENCE INSTEAD OF ESTIMATE.

Nobody knows what eleven hundred names actually contain. How many are documented well enough to quiz. How many are contested. How many are one anecdote in a Victorian encyclopaedia repeated for a century. Every estimate anyone could give would be a guess dressed as a number, and this ship has learned what those cost.

The halt log answers it by walking the whole roster and reporting what it found. Not an opinion about the library — a reading of it. That is the quiet prize, and it is worth more than the quizzes, because it is the only thing that can tell you what you actually have.

V · The ordnance

Which brings the matter to its proper end, and to the reason this brief does not close with a summary.

Everything above could be written down. A document could hold the modes and the tiers, the counts and the costs, and it would be accurate this morning. It would be wrong by Thursday, and it would look exactly the same.

That has already happened here. Five briefs on this shelf stated that the library held eight quizzes. It held thirteen, and had for weeks. Not one of those documents was careless. Every one was true when written, and every one went on being read after it stopped being true — because a document cannot tell you it has gone stale. It has no way to know.

A DOCUMENT LIES BY STANDING STILL.
AN INSTRUMENT GOES DARK RATHER THAN SAY SOMETHING IT CANNOT SEE.

The Ordnance Bay already knows this. It was built to answer one question — what has this ship actually fired? — and it answers by reading the manifest every time it is opened. When the reading fails it says so in plain words: I AM SHOWING YOU NOTHING. I am not showing you the last thing I saw. Five tubes publish from this ship, and the Bay is the only place their firing is visible.

Figure 2 · five tubes, and the sixth this brief proposes.

The quiz library is a sixth tube. It fires rounds — forty-six live, seven hundred and eighty-six in the magazine, three loaded every hour without a hand on the lever. It has calibres: d1 the entry, d2 another charge, d3 the contested. It has three kinds of round: life, canon, source. And it has a firing log that no other tube possesses — a record not only of what went out but of every target the ship examined and refused, with the reason.

So it should not be described in a document. It should be installed in the bay, beside the four publishing tubes, reading the table every time the pane is opened. The count becomes a reading rather than a claim. The halt log becomes a dial. And on the day the table cannot be reached, the bay does what it was built to do and goes dark, rather than showing a number from last week and letting a captain believe it.

We have come this far. The engine researches, verifies, refuses, and writes without either of us present. Its output should not be filed on a shelf where it will quietly go out of date.

IT SHOULD BECOME PART OF THE SHIP.

VI · What remains

Move State 

The cron trigger — 0 * * * *, three figures an hour one setting, not yet set 

The sixth tube in the Ordnance Bay this brief proposes it 

Depth 2 and 3 across the stack the engine is ready 

The Gates — six marks, and the next opens specified · half built 

The scoring animation specified 

Leif Erikson still has one quiz. Depth one, no reading room, written from public sources on an afternoon when the question was whether it could be done at all.

Forty-five quizzes later, the answer is on the table, and seven hundred and forty-nine names are waiting for the same crossing.

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · THE LEIF BRIEF · 2026-07-19 · 09:40 UTC · Rev. A


==============================================================================
  BRIEF 23 OF 50  ·  THE LIKENESS · AMENTI
==============================================================================
  file     Amenti_The_Likeness.html
  written  2026-07-23
  words    1,958
==============================================================================

THE LIKENESS · Amenti
· 

Implementation Brief·The Shelf·Ingram Manor LLC

23 July 2026·2026-07-23·Rev. A

Amenti.live · What Is Drawn, And When

TheLIKENESS

Four Owners · One Description · The Refusal of the Icon

STYLE holds that the description is the asset and the image is a cache. There is no description. Twenty-one hand-built SVGs are the only record of what any figure in this library looks like — which makes the cache the asset, and inverts the law that everything else rests on. This brief closes that gap, and closes a second one beside it: left to itself an engine draws the icon, and the icon is almost never the moment.

THE ICON IS THE STATISTICAL MODE OF THE TRAINING DATA.
THE PIVOTAL MOMENT IS ALMOST NEVER THE MODE.

1 · The evidence, computed from the live library

Age at the moment is not a stylistic preference. It is arithmetic on two numbers the fleet already holds — Birth-Date in the ledger, and the year in the quiz payload — and it is the single largest source of wrong images in the system today.

Figure Quiz Age then What a model draws unprompted 

Helen Keller keller-water 7 the dignified adult author 

Albert Einstein einstein-1905 26 white hair, tongue out, aged 68 

Nikola Tesla tesla-brush-discharge-1892 36 an old man with pigeons 

Christopher Columbus columbus-chains 49 triumphant on a prow 

Galileo Galilei galileo-recantation 69 a bearded sage at a telescope 

Keller was seven years old at the pump. Every image in the model's head is the adult. Draw the icon and you have not made a stylistic error — you have illustrated the wrong person, on the exact moment the quiz is about.

Columbus is the sharper case. He is not merely the wrong age in the icon, he is the wrong register: the quiz is about being arrested and shipped home in irons, and the icon is a conqueror on a prow. That render would contradict its own passage.

2 · Four owners, no overlap

Everything below follows from keeping these separate. Where two of them own the same fact, the fact drifts.

Figure 1 · four owners, one cache.

3 · The ledger columns

All additive. None touches an existing column, and therefore none re-renders a single second of cached audio &#8212; see &#167;8.

Column Kind Carries 

Appearance prose, 1&#8211;2 sentences build, hair, beard, marks. Body only &#8212; never clothing. 

AppearanceBasis enum photograph &#183; depicted &#183; described &#183; tradition &#183; unknown 

Dress prose the signature costume, the one that reads as them 

Signature short the identifying object: the elephant, the top hat, the two swords 

Accent hex theirs, at every hour of the day (see R05) 

Glyph one char the mark, for when no face exists 

Epithet short &#8220;The Crosser&#8221;, &#8220;Lion of Carthage&#8221; &#8212; the card&#8217;s second line 

Cliche short what NOT to draw. &#8220;no tongue, no wild hair &#8212; he is 26 and dark-haired&#8221; 

Why the basis is required and not optional

For most of a thousand figures, nobody knows what they looked like. Accuracy therefore cannot mean one thing, and the fleet already owns the right structure for this: life, canon and source, applied now to the face rather than the record.

unknown is a correct value, not a failure. &#8220;No reliable likeness survives&#8221; instructs the renderer to lean on dress and the signature object &#8212; and Hannibal is recognised by the elephant and the helmet, not by his cheekbones.

The cautionary case is already in the file. The Moses portrait carries <!-- HORN-LIGHT (the iconography from Michelangelo's Moses) -->. Those horns are a mistranslation &#8212; Hebrew keren, radiance, read as horns &#8212; that became eight centuries of art. Drawing them is RIGHT, because the subject is the tradition's Moses. Only a basis field lets the fleet say so honestly instead of asserting a likeness it does not have.

4 · The scene record

A scene is style-agnostic by construction: it says what is happening, never how it is drawn. One scene per quiz is nearly free &#8212; the engine has just researched that moment &#8212; plus whatever is written by hand. One per figure is marked primary: the card face.

scene_key · figure_key · topic_id? · primary
moment what is happening, as an ACTION IN PROGRESS
age integer — quiz year minus Birth-Date, REQUIRED
shot portrait | medium | wide
angle front | three-quarter | profile | over-shoulder | low | high
set void | chamber | field | court | shore | study | street |
threshold | height | cell
time day | twilight | night | firelit | overcast
place interior | exterior
dress optional override — Caesar in the field is not Caesar in the Senate

Enums, not prose

set &#183; time &#183; place &#183; shot &#183; angle

Prose is where stereotype hides, because a model fills an unspecified field with its mode. An enumerated field cannot be filled with a mode; it can only be chosen, and a wrong choice is visible in a table.

The three planes

background silhouette only, flat, one or two tones. Pillars, treeline, crowd. Never detailed. 

midground the figure, fully rendered. Today this is the ONLY plane that exists. 

foreground one cropped, single-tone element. A spear butt, a table edge, a shoulder. 

The foreground plane is the cheapest depth available in vector and the one currently missing entirely. It also gives the style validator something concrete to enforce: background may not use the skin gradient; foreground is single-tone.

5 · The refusals

The validator is not a review &#8212; it is a parser. Each rule below is machine-checkable, and a scene that fails one never reaches the renderer.

· A scene must name an age. Computed, not guessed: quiz year &#8722; Birth-Date. A scene with no age is refused. This one rule removes most of the icon problem.

· A scene must contain an action in progress and an object in a named state. &#8220;At a desk, thoughtful&#8221; is refused. &#8220;Tamping the pipe, the match flaring&#8221; passes. Churchill&#8217;s cigar has gone out; the tablets are mid-smash, not dropped.

· Prefer the adjacent moment. The Rubicon is not the crossing &#8212; it is the pause on the bank. The recantation is not the kneeling &#8212; it is standing up afterward. The instant before or after holds the decision rather than the outcome.

· No repeat within a figure&#8217;s stack. Before writing, read that figure&#8217;s existing scenes and forbid the same age band, shot, set and time. Four gates must yield four genuinely different images by rule. This is existingFor, pointed at scenes instead of moments.

· The accent belongs to the figure and survives the light. Light the SET; never repaint the figure. If twilight recolours everyone, every figure looks the same at twilight and the one colour that identifies them is gone.

· Honour the tradition&#8217;s own terms. Where a tradition forbids an image, draw the moment &#8212; the document, the city, the covenant &#8212; and leave the figure out. That is not a lesser portrait; it is the correct one.

· No caricature. Draw the person doing the thing they are known for, in the dress of their own culture as its own people would render it, at the age they were. The specificity that defeats cliché is the specificity that defeats caricature; they fail together and they are fixed together.

· A scene must teach. If the image only restates the caption it is decoration. Caesar working through two stacks of documents is the dictatorship in one gesture. A seven-year-old at a pump is the quiz.

SEEK UNIQUENESS IN THE CONTENT. SEEK COHESION IN THE STYLE.
VARY THE MOMENT, THE AGE, THE CAMERA, THE LIGHT.
NEVER VARY THE SPEC.

The twenty-one puppets read as one cast precisely because they share defs(), the 320&#215;560 stage, and the pool of light. The moment one figure earns a bespoke treatment because they &#8220;deserve&#8221; it, the library becomes a folder of illustrations. That constraint is the asset.

6 · The reading room

A figure with four gates has four moments &#8212; a visual through-line of a life rather than one static portrait. The primary scene is the card face; the rest belong to the room. This also gives depth-2 and depth-3 quizzes something they presently lack: a reason to exist visually, and not merely as further questions.

7 · The build

Likeness IThe Columnsfirst · additive · free

Eight columns on the ledger. Nothing edited, nothing renamed, no consumer changed.

Done when
The Sheet carries the columns and the curated 38 are filled by hand as the reference set.

Likeness IIThe Engine Fills Themthe patrol

SHEET_SPEC gains appearance, appearanceBasis, dress, signature and cliche. The engine already researches the figure with search; these come out of the same pass at no extra call.

Done when
A fresh run returns a basis of unknown for Hannibal and photograph for Keller, and refuses to assert a likeness it cannot source.

Likeness IIIScenesone per quiz

The scenes table, the enums, and the eight refusals as a validator. Backfill: every existing portrait becomes that figure&#8217;s primary scene, shot=portrait, set=void.

Done when
Caesar holds two scenes that differ in age band, shot and set &#8212; and the validator refuses a third that repeats any of them.

Likeness IVMangasample three before committing

Manga is a VECTOR style &#8212; line art, flat tones, screentones, no gradients &#8212; which is what SVG does best and what the existing pipeline already produces. A new spec and a new validator; no provider, no bucket, no licensing question.

Sample Caesar, Rand and Gilgamesh: a fighter, a thinker, a giant. If the style cannot hold a woman at 14 combat holding a manuscript, it is not a style for this library.

Done when
Three figures render from description alone, recognisably the same people as their puppets.

Likeness VRetire, Never Deleteone word

Puppet becomes status='retired' in the styles table. The thirty-eight SVGs stay in the drawer. They are paid for, they work, and they are the fallback while manga sits below coverage.

Note
A style is offered at 100% coverage and at no other number. Manga at 40 of 999 does not qualify, and retiring puppet does not change that. Retirement is a display decision; coverage is a rendering programme.

DO NOT RENDER FORTY MORE PUPPETS.
ENGINE TIME SPENT ON A CACHE YOU INTEND TO DISCARD
IS THE ONLY BUDGET HERE THAT CANNOT BE RECOVERED.

8 · What must not be touched

Voice, Dialect and Gender are inside the audio cache key. composeStyle() folds dialect and voice into the /speak style string, and audioKey = sha256(TTS_MODEL + voice + STYLE + TEXT). Change one byte of a figure&#8217;s dialect and every clip ever rendered for them is orphaned: the archive keeps them, no invoice mentions the loss, and the calibration passage will report the drift.

ADDING A COLUMN IS FREE.
EDITING VOICE, DIALECT OR GENDER RE-BILLS THE ARCHIVE.
ENRICH BY ADDITION.

If the twenty-one-value voice vocabulary proves too coarse, do not rewrite it. Add VoiceNotes, and have composeStyle append it only for figures that carry one &#8212; so every unchanged figure keeps every cached second.

9 · Acceptance

the description test Delete a rendering and it regenerates recognisably. If it does not, the description is not the asset yet. 

the age test Ask for keller-water and receive a seven-year-old. 

the stack test A figure with four gates yields four images that differ in age, shot and set &#8212; by rule, not by luck. 

the cast test Thirty renders side by side read as one library, not thirty illustrations. 

the honesty test Every drawn face can name its basis, and the ones that cannot say unknown. 

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · THE LIKENESS · implementation brief · 2026-07-23 · Rev. A

THE QUIZ SURFACE · Amenti Engineering Brief
· 

Amenti.live · Engineering Brief · The Shelf

TheQUIZ SURFACE

Forty-eight hours · the engine, the bridge, and the cards

Engineering Brief·Ingram Manor LLC·Amenti.live

19 July 2026 · 07:12 UTC·2026-07-19·Rev. A

In forty-eight hours the quiz surface went from thirteen hand-written quizzes locked inside a money Worker to a self-generating library of thirty-two, a roster that builds itself from live data, thirty-three complete character cards, and a machine that researches and writes new quizzes without either author present. This brief records what was built, what broke, and what the breakages taught.

32
Quizzes Live
from 13

33
Complete Cards
art · sheet · words · quiz

12
Portraits Drawn
library 21 → 33

$0.11
Cost Per Quiz
measured, not estimated

I · What the surface was

At the start, everything lived in one place. The amenti-mint Worker held the quiz content, the answer keys, the scoring logic and the ledger writes in a single 66 KB file. Adding a quiz meant editing and redeploying the one piece of infrastructure that moves money. The arena showed six hand-typed cards in front of that library, and the two lists had already drifted — a card claimed "5 QUESTIONS" for a quiz that held four, and gave Lincoln an era his own quiz contradicted.

Worse, the count was wrong in the documents. Five briefs stated the library held eight quizzes. It held thirteen. The number had been carried forward from a summary and never checked against the source.

A DOCUMENT THAT IS NEVER CHECKED AGAINST THE SHIP
BECOMES A CONFIDENT DESCRIPTION OF SOMETHING THAT NO LONGER EXISTS.

II · The migration

The first structural change moved the quiz library out of the money Worker and into a topics table. It was done in four steps, deliberately, because the Worker that scores answers also writes to the ledger.

Figure 1 · before and after. The library left the Worker; the keys did not.

The order mattered more than the change. Copy first, verify the copy, then delete. A migration route was pasted into the live Worker so that it copied its own quizzes into the table — nothing retyped, nothing mistranscribed. Only once the count matched on both sides was the constant removed.

Step What it did Verified by 

1 Create the topics table, RLS on, no policies 0 rows, no error 

2 Temporary route copies in-code TOPICS into the table 18 in table · 18 in code 

3 Console fires the migration and reads it back ids matched exactly 

4 Slim Worker reads the table; constant deleted Worker 66KB → 31KB 

The proof the migration had truly happened came from an unexpected place: the JSON key order changed. Postgres jsonb sorts object keys by length then alphabetically, so figure, domain, motif… came back as era, motif, domain…. Code cannot do that. The data had made a round trip through the database.

The result is the point: adding a quiz is now one row insert. Four hours later, nine quizzes were added without Cloudflare being opened at all.

III · The anatomy of a quiz

The format was already in production and did not need designing — it needed writing down. Each quiz is a verified passage and six questions across three cognitive tiers, ending in the pair that feeds the court.

Figure 2 · the invariant. Every quiz, without exception.

Two rules emerged from writing them by hand that no document had captured:

The evaluate pair is not always champion-and-tyrant. For a ruler it is great deeds against tyranny. For a reformer it is the inspiring myth against the erased reality — Helen Keller's pump against the socialist the story forgets. For an explorer it is the robbed pioneer against the idea that "discovery" erases the people already there. Forcing one frame on every figure produces false quizzes.

The pans are cases, not questions. Each must begin "Make the strongest case that…" The court scores the quality of an argument, and a yes/no question cannot elicit one. This rule was learned when the engine returned "Was Einstein's 1905 work humanity's greatest achievement?" — structurally valid, and it breaks the entire mechanic.

IV · The Quizzard

The bottleneck was never the format. It was that every quiz came out of a conversation. The Quizzard is a Cloudflare Worker that takes one name and returns a validated quiz or an honest refusal.

Figure 3 · six gates and a halt rail. Every gate can refuse.

The load-bearing detail is that the model is called with the web search tool armed. A model writing from memory is precisely the fabrication risk the doctrine exists to prevent; search is what makes verification real rather than decorative.

The validator is a parser, not a review

Every returned quiz is checked in the Worker against the invariant — never by the model, never by a human first. Tested both directions before it was trusted: it accepts all five hand-written quizzes, and rejects nine deliberate corruptions including a dropped question, an out-of-range answer index, a tampered rubric and a third timed question.

Normalise constants, validate content

Three fields carry no information — the timed tiers, the philosophical word limit, the rubric. They are identical in every quiz ever written. Requiring a model to recall a constant, and discarding a researched quiz when it forgets, is friction with no benefit. The Worker supplies those three and nothing else: never a fact, never an option, never an answer index. Everything it fills is reported.

A HALT IS A SUCCESS, NOT A FAILURE.
THE ENGINE THAT CANNOT SAY NO WILL EVENTUALLY SAY SOMETHING FALSE.

What it refused

Input Verdict Why it matters 

Leif Erikson produced no reading room, only a roster row — proof depth is optional 

Homer halted contested existence; no biography can be asserted 

nine names pasted as one halted caught before the API call — the mispaste cost nothing 

Frederick Douglass ×2 halted, then produced passage 211 then 170 words against a 165 ceiling 

V · The Bridge

A Worker with a secret-guarded POST route is unreachable from a browser address bar. The bridge is the console that drives it — and it is the only place the engine's work becomes visible: searches run, tokens spent, sources retrieved, and the raw response one click away.

It was first delivered as a file to download. That was a mistake, and it cost four rounds. A stale copy had no batch panel, so nine names pasted into a single-line field arrived as one sixteen-word figure. The Worker now serves its own console at its root, with no-store, so the tool and the engine cannot drift apart.

The bug beneath the bug

Even after the batch panel existed, every paste still collapsed. The cause was in the embedding: escaping every backslash turned split('\n') into split('\\n') — a literal backslash-n that never matches. The same escaping mangled ten unicode symbol escapes. The bridge contained no backticks and no interpolation, so it never needed escaping at all.

Probe 19 had reported eight green passes throughout, because it tested whether the textarea held nine lines. It did. The fault was one step later, in the splitter.

A PROBE THAT CHECKS THE COMPONENT NEXT TO THE BROKEN ONE
IS WORSE THAN NO PROBE — IT CERTIFIES THE FAILURE.

VI · The card

A card is not a name. Four artifacts, in four places, and three of four is a gap wearing a border.

Figure 4 · the four artifacts and where each lives.

The arena's cards were hand-written while the library was data — two lists maintained separately, already disagreeing. They are now generated from the live library: fetch /quiz/topics, build a card per quiz, attach the click handlers, then paint the portraits from the art library and the stat bars from the codex. Six cards became thirty-two, and drift became impossible.

Three failures worth recording

A card with nothing behind it served a stranger's quiz. Clicking Lincoln opened Caesar, because data-topic || DEFAULT_TOPIC substituted silently. A card with no quiz now says so and offers a picker of what is ready.

Ten characters were written, pasted correctly, and did not appear. AMENTI_CHARS was declared three separate times in Page1 — once each for the codex, timeline and terminal blocks. The last to run overwrote the others. AMENTI_ERAS had the same fault. Both are now declared once, and the page shed 62 KB.

The portraits were never six. A first attempt read .char-slide .char-art — the six hero-carousel slides — and concluded six figures were drawn. The real library was window.AMENTI_SVG: twenty-one hand-built portraits keyed by codex key. The correction doubled the faces on the roster before a single new one was drawn.

IF A LIST IS DECLARED TWICE, ONE OF THEM IS A LIE WAITING TO BE TOLD.

VII · The art migration

Twelve portraits were drawn to close the gap — Douglass, Keller, Prometheus, Loki, Odysseus, Gilgamesh, Arthur, Lycurgus, Manlius, Rand, Leif and Ingram. Each is a hand-built SVG on the house 320×560 stage, matching the head geometry and shared defs() of the twenty-one that came before, with accent colours read from each figure's codex record rather than invented.

They were first delivered as blocks to paste into Page1. That was the wrong instruction and the captain said so: art belongs in files that grow, not pasted into a 1.6 MB page every time. They now live in amenti-art-2.js, which attaches to the library Page1 created — or creates it if it loads first, so load order cannot break it — and refreshes the roster once the art is in.

Where Portraits Note 

Page1.html 21 the original library; left in place, it works 

amenti-art-2.js 12 new file; every future portrait appends here 

total 33 every codex character now has a face 

Each was drawn from its own stat line rather than a generic pose. Gilgamesh — 96 combat, 66 foresight — stands with his back to the walls the epic offers as his true monument. Rand, at 14 combat the smallest fighter in the set, carries a cigarette holder and a manuscript. Odysseus is roped to his own mast, because he wanted to hear the song and survive it, so he arranged both.

VIII · The Corps turned on itself

The Probe Corps exists to cross-examine claims against the living system. It was the one thing nothing was checking.

The scanner that catalogues the fleet excluded probes by name and never walked probes/ at all: nineteen files on disk, one in the manifest, and a pane claiming twenty in rows typed by hand. It could have claimed any number.

Both ends are fixed. The scan now catalogues the folder and reads each probe's codename from its own header; the pane renders that manifest instead of a remembered list, and goes dark rather than guessing if it cannot be read.

Before After 

Probe files on disk 19 20 

Known to the manifest 1 20 

Pane roster typed read 

Eight of the twenty report "codename unread" rather than being listed as nameless. That is the scanner admitting blindness, not asserting absence — the third rule of the doctrine, applied to the instrument that enforces it.

IX · The ledger of the forty-eight hours

Measure Before After 

Quizzes live 13 32 

Written by machine 0 11 

Quiz library location in the mint a table 

Mint Worker size 66 KB 31 KB 

Adding a quiz edit + deploy one insert 

Arena cards 6 typed 32 generated 

Codex records 23 33 

Portraits 21 33 

Complete cards 12 32 

Character list declarations 3 1 

Probes in the manifest 1 20 

X · What the failures taught

Nine faults were found and fixed. Every one was visible to anyone who looked, and none required insight.

Fault Found by 

Five briefs stating 8 quizzes; there were 13 reading the Worker 

Cards without a quiz opening Caesar clicking Lincoln 

Three copies of the character list grepping for the assignment 

Portrait library found at 6, actually 21 the captain saying so 

Batch splitter matching a literal backslash reading the embedded source 

A probe certifying a broken splitter auditing the probe 

A probe asserting an unverified cause auditing the probe 

Search counter unable to tell diligence from fabrication a run reporting zero 

Douglass filed as Early Modern reading the output 

The last one is the instructive one. "Early Modern" is a legal value in the controlled vocabulary — the validator passed it. A vocabulary check catches invented words, not wrong choices among real ones. That class of error is exactly why quizzes are written as staged and promoted by hand.

XI · What remains

Ingram has no quiz, and that is the correct gap: a living founder is precisely the case the doctrine says not to put on trial.

Canon mode is specified and unarmed. Roughly a hundred and fifty figures — the mythic tier, the legendary founders, the scriptures — sit behind it. The engine refuses mode: canon until the interpretive frames for religious figures are written, which is the single place in the fleet where autonomy is withheld by design.

The patrol is unbuilt. The engine runs when a button is pressed. The queue, the staging table and the schedule that would let it work down the roster unattended are drawn but not made.

THE CAPTAIN IS NOT A COMPONENT.
EVERY STAGE THAT STILL REQUIRES HIM IS A STAGE NOT YET FINISHED.

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · THE QUIZ SURFACE · engineering brief · 2026-07-19 · 07:12 UTC · Rev. A

  BRIEF 25 OF 50  ·  THE QUIZZARD · AMENTI

THE QUIZZARD · Amenti

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · The Generator Specification

The Quizzard

The station that turns a roster row into a trial. It reads a name from the dead, goes and learns the history, verifies it, and returns a complete quiz in the Worker's own schema — a court target, an ET faucet, and a relic, in one pass.

THE CAPTAIN IS NOT A COMPONENT.
A SYSTEM THAT NEEDS ITS FOUNDER TO PICK THE NEXT NAME IS NOT SELF-SUSTAINING.

1 · What the Quizzard is for

The economy rests on the quizzes: each one is simultaneously a court target (the champion/tyrant pair becomes the case argued), an ET faucet (mastery mints), and a relic anchor. With eight quizzes, the whole platform stands on eight people. The roster holds 893 verifiable dead. The gap between those numbers is the entire problem, and hand-authoring cannot close it — not by the captain, not by an assistant writing batches in a chat window.

The Quizzard is the answer: a documented, repeatable procedure precise enough that a script, a future model, or a stranger who never met the founder can run it and produce a quiz that meets the bar. Human review becomes a check, never a component.

2 · The Input — one roster row

The queue is names.csv, deny-filtered, dead only (death-date is not the 2026 placeholder). One row is the whole input:

Rank, Full Name, Title, Birth-Date, Death-Date, Biography, Links, Gender, Dialect, Voice, Region, Location
501, Leif Erikson, Explorer, 970, 1020, "...", ..., Northern Europe, ...

A library folder is NOT required. Lincoln has a full reading room; Leif has only this row. Both produce equally good quizzes, because the substance comes from verified research, not from pre-existing depth. The row is the anchor; history is the source.

3 · The Procedure — six steps, in order

Step 1 · Identify — resolve the figure to ONE real person
Confirm the name maps to a single, unambiguous historical individual. If it does not — a name shared by several figures, a conflation, a legend of contested existence — stop and flag; do not proceed on a guess.

Proven in practice: "Claudius Manlius" resolved to no one (Claudia and Manlia are different gentes). The run halted rather than inventing a person. Homer would halt here too — a figure who may never have existed cannot have his life asserted as fact.

Step 2 · Research — find the pivotal moment
Search real sources. Find the figure's turning point — the Rubicon: one moment where the life bends and the legacy is decided. Not a career summary; a scene.

Caesar at the river. Confucius resigning over a portion of sacrificial meat. Manlius thrown from the rock he saved. Keller at the pump. Leif on the shore of Vinland. For a figure of ideas rather than events, the pivot is the work — Rand writing Atlas Shrugged.

Step 3 · Verify — every fact that will become an answer key
Confirm each load-bearing fact against real sources before writing any key. Names, dates, places, titles, outcomes. Never write a key from memory.

A WRONG ANSWER KEY POISONS THE ECONOMY.
A SEEKER WHO KNOWS THE HISTORY AND IS MARKED WRONG NEVER TRUSTS THE LEDGER AGAIN.

This is the probe doctrine applied to content: measure, do not assume. It is the speed limit of the entire pipeline, and it is not negotiable — the alternative is fifty quizzes that read well and quietly lie.

Step 4 · Find the DUALITY — the one creative act
Every quiz turns on two opposing cases a real person could honestly argue. This is the heart of the court, and the frame must fit the figure — forcing one shape on everyone produces false quizzes.

Figure type The duality that fits Proven in 

Ruler / general Great deeds vs. tyranny manlius-capitol 

Philosopher in power Sage of principle vs. failed idealist confucius-lu 

Reformer / icon The inspiring myth vs. the erased reality keller-water 

Thinker of ideas The ideology's promise vs. its cost rand-atlas 

Explorer Pioneer robbed of credit vs. "discovery" as myth leif-vinland 

The test: can a thoughtful person honestly argue both sides from real history? If one side is empty — if there is only atrocity, or only praise — the figure has nothing to weigh. Flag and skip.

Fairness rule: write the strongest version of each case, drawn from how the world actually argues the figure. Never strawman the side you find less sympathetic. The rubric scores the quality of the argument, never the conclusion.

Step 5 · Compose — fill the fixed schema
The structure is invariant. Only the content changes.

Step 6 · Validate — machine-check before emit
Parse as JSON. Confirm the invariant (below). Reject and re-run on failure. A quiz that does not validate never reaches the Worker.

4 · The Invariant — what every quiz must satisfy

topic id kebab-case, figure + moment e.g. "leif-vinland"
title "Figure and the Moment" e.g. "Manlius and the Tarpeian Rock"
era "PERIOD · YEAR" e.g. "VIKING AGE · c. 1000 AD"
facets figure[], domain[], motif[], region[], era[], sourceType
sourceType = "Event" | "Idea"
intro 1-2 sentences. The hook. States the tension, not the answer.
passage 115-155 words of VERIFIED history. The source of truth for every key.

questions EXACTLY 6, in this shape:
cognitive 3 x what · 1 x why · 2 x evaluate
answerType 3 x mc · 1 x cloze · 2 x philosophical
timed 2 (on easy recall MC only) tiers { full:6, partial:20 }
difficulty ~3 easy · 1 medium · 2 hard

mc 4 options, "correct" index, distractors plausible but clearly wrong
cloze "accepted" array — include spelling/format variants
philosophical wordLimit 40, the champion/tyrant pair, rubric on both:
"No single correct verdict — score the QUALITY of the case,
not the conclusion."

5 · The Motif vocabulary

Each figure gets one motif — the theme their story carries. Established so far, extend as needed:

Motif Carries 

Ambition & Its Cost The reach that consumes the reacher (Caesar) 

Principle & Its Price Integrity that costs everything (Confucius) 

Glory & Its Ruin The hero undone by his own honours (Manlius) 

The Myth & The Person The sanitized story vs. the real life (Keller) 

The Self & The Others The individual against the collective (Rand) 

The First & The Forgotten Priority lost to another's fame (Leif) 

6 · The Halt Conditions — when the Quizzard must refuse

A generator that never refuses will fabricate. These are mandatory stops:

· Ambiguous identity — the name resolves to more than one person, or none. (Claudius Manlius)

· Contested existence — the figure may be legend. Facts cannot be asserted about a life that may not have happened. (Homer)

· Living person — death-date is the 2026 placeholder. The heart cannot be weighed while the life is unfinished. (the Living Legends antechamber)

· Empty pan — no honest case exists on one side. Nothing to weigh.

· Unverifiable pivot — the turning point cannot be confirmed in real sources. Do not invent a scene.

A halt is a success, not a failure. The Quizzard that returns "cannot verify — skipped" is working exactly as designed. The one that returns a confident fabrication is the disaster.

7 · Reference implementations

Five complete quizzes, authored by this procedure, are the standard the Quizzard must match. Each covers a different figure-type and a different duality — together they prove the schema holds across the whole range of the roster.

Topic id Figure type Motif Source Duality 

confucius-lu Philosopher in power Principle & Its Price Event sage / failed idealist 

manlius-capitol Soldier-populist Glory & Its Ruin Event champion / tyrant 

keller-water Reformer The Myth & The Person Event icon / erased radical 

rand-atlas Thinker of ideas The Self & The Others Idea prophet / rationalizer 

leif-vinland Explorer The First & The Forgotten Event pioneer / myth 

Note: leif-vinland is the load-bearing proof — Leif has no library folder, only a roster row. The quiz is complete and verified regardless. Depth is optional; research is not.

8 · Toward the autonomous Quizzard

This document is the specification. The implementation follows it in stages, each useful on its own:

· 1 Manual runs — the procedure followed by hand. Done: five quizzes.

· 2 Batch runs — the procedure applied down the roster by rank, emitting a paste-ready TOPICS file.

· 3 The queue — a work-list file tracking which roster rows are done, halted (with reason), and pending. The Quizzard's memory.

· 4 The Worker — amenti-quizzard: takes a roster row, calls a model with this spec as its system prompt plus search for verification, validates the output against the invariant, emits or halts.

· 5 The patrol — the Quizzard runs on a schedule, working down the queue, adding trials to the docket while no one watches. The fleet writes its own court.

THE ROSTER IS THE QUEUE. THE SCHEMA IS FIXED. THE RESEARCH IS THE WORK.
THE ONLY CREATIVE ACT IS FINDING THE TWO HONEST CASES.

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · THE QUIZZARD · generator specification · 2026-07-18

THE SACRED FRAMES · Amenti
· 

Decision Document·The Shelf·Ingram Manor LLC

19 July 2026·2026-07-19·Rev. B · RULED — NOBODY LEFT OUT

Amenti.live · The Gate on Canon Mode

TheSACRED FRAMES

Four Frames · Two Classes · Nobody Left Out

Canon mode opens roughly a hundred and fifty figures the engine currently refuses. It stays shut until these frames exist, because this is the one place where a wrong duality does not produce a weak quiz — it produces an insult.

NO GENERATOR IMPROVISES A DUALITY FOR A RELIGIOUS FIGURE.
THE ENGINE SELECTS FROM THESE FRAMES. IT NEVER INVENTS ONE.

Why this is drafted, not decided

The Cannons brief withholds one thing from the engine: the frame for a sacred figure. That does not mean these were yours to write from nothing — it means the engine may not invent one at runtime, per figure, unsupervised. A frame set written in advance, read and edited by you, and selected from at runtime is exactly what the brief asks for.

So: below is a draft. Nothing is armed until you have edited and approved it. Cut a frame you dislike, rewrite a pan you would not want argued in your hall, and the engine will only ever use what survives.

The distinction that governs everything

The roster holds two very different kinds of sacred figure, and treating them alike is the first mistake available.

Dead sacred Living sacred 

Who Apollo · Prometheus · Odin · Loki · Osiris · Enlil · Inanna · Gilgamesh Muhammad · Jesus · Buddha · Moses · Abraham · Krishna · Guru Nanak · Zoroaster 

Adherents today none billions 

Treatment the ordinary canon duality; already proven on Prometheus and Loki a named frame from this document, or a halt 

This classification cannot be automated. An attempt to sort the roster by name matched Isaac Newton against Isaac, Abraham Lincoln against Abraham, and Adam Smith against Adam. Which is the argument for a written list rather than a clever rule.

The four frames

Each is a real argument, and each is one the tradition has with itself. That is the test: a frame a devout reader would recognise as a fair statement of a dispute they have heard before — not one only an outsider would call fair.

Frame AThe Text and Its Readings

Two readings of the same passage, both held within the tradition. The oldest and safest frame, because the argument is already in the commentary.

Pan onethe reading that vindicates the act in its own terms

Pan twothe reading that finds it hard, as the tradition itself has

PROVEN: moses-calf — the liberator whose severity held a fragile people together, against the leader who answered his own people's fear with slaughter. Both are arguments Jewish commentary has made for two thousand years.

Frame BThe Teaching and What Was Built On It

What the figure is recorded as saying, against the institution raised in their name. Every tradition has reformers who made exactly this argument — it is internal, not hostile.

Pan onethe institution carried the teaching further than one life could

Pan twothe institution departed from the teaching it claims

CAUTION: this frame is only honest when both pans are about the INSTITUTION. The moment pan two becomes "the teaching itself was wrong," it has left the frame.

Frame CThe Hard Passage

A text the tradition openly wrestles with. Not a text chosen to embarrass — a text its own scholars have written libraries about because it is difficult.

Pan onewhat the passage demands, taken at its word

Pan twowhat it costs, taken equally seriously

CANDIDATE: the binding of Isaac — obedience as the summit of faith, against a father who raised the knife. Kierkegaard wrote a book on precisely this tension. It is not an attack; it is the argument.

Frame DThe Historical and the Remembered

What the documentary record can establish, against what the tradition holds. A genuine scholarly frame with two centuries behind it.

Pan onethe record supports far more than sceptics allow

Pan twothe remembered figure grew well past what the record carries

CAUTION: this frame asks about EVIDENCE, never about truth. "What can be documented" is a fair question. "Whether the faith is true" is not one this hall asks.

The refusal

A frame set is only as good as its willingness to produce nothing. If none of the four fits a figure without wounding, the engine halts — and a halt costs nothing.

Hard limits · binding on every canon quiz

· Never ask whether a religion is true. The hall weighs texts and figures. It does not adjudicate faith.

· Never ask a seeker to argue that believers are foolish. A pan written that way is not a case; it is a slur with a word limit.

· Both pans must be recognisable to a devout reader as fair statements of a real dispute. If only an outsider would call it balanced, it is not.

· The passage stays in the register of the text. "As the book records it," never a flat historical assertion.

· If no approved frame fits, HALT. Reason: no-fitting-frame. Skip the figure; do not improvise.

What arming this opens

Band Treatment Roughly 

Mythic tier — dead sacred ordinary canon duality, no frame needed 99 

Legendary founders ordinary canon duality Romulus · Theseus · Agamemnon · Hippolyta 

Scripture — living sacred Frames A–D only Isaac · Jacob · Joseph · Aaron · Elijah · David 

Authorship questions Idea mode, not Canon the Homeric Question 

The ruling · nobody is left out

The draft offered to hold the highest-ranked figures back from the first run. That was declined, and the reasoning is sound: a hall that weighs the dead but flinches at the most consequential of them is not weighing anything. Muhammad at rank one and Jesus at rank three are not exceptions to the library — they are the reason it exists.

This raises the standard rather than lowering it. Including them means the frames must be good enough to survive a reader who holds the figure sacred. Below is what that looks like in practice, stated concretely so it is a specification and not a sentiment.

Rank 1Muhammad

Frame A · The Text and Its Readings. Anchor on a documented episode, not on the person in general. The Constitution of Medina is the strongest candidate: a document historians across traditions treat as genuine, and one Muslim scholarship discusses at length.

Pan onethe statesman who bound warring tribes and a Jewish community into one polity under a written covenant — governance by agreement where there had been feud

Pan twothe tension every prophet-statesman inherits: that revelation and rule in one hand makes disagreement with the ruler into disagreement with God

Both pans are arguments made inside Islamic political thought. Neither asks whether the revelation is true.

Rank 3Jesus

Frame D · The Historical and the Remembered. The frame with two centuries of scholarship behind it, and the one most Christian seminaries teach directly.

Pan onethe record carries more than sceptics allow — Paul writing within twenty years and meeting the brother, Tacitus and Josephus attesting from outside, the baptism and the crucifixion among the most secure facts in ancient history

Pan twothe remembered figure grew past what the record carries, and separating the two is the work of every honest historian of the period

This asks about EVIDENCE. It does not ask whether the resurrection happened, and it never will.

Rank 4The Buddha

Frame A, anchored on the renunciation: a prince who left a wife and infant son to seek an end to suffering.

Pan onethe departure that made the teaching possible, and has eased suffering for two and a half thousand years

Pan twoa man who walked out on a wife and a newborn — a cost Buddhist commentary itself has never pretended away

The Buddhist tradition addresses this directly. Raising it is not an attack; ignoring it would be the flattery.

The pattern in all three: anchor on a documented episode, never on the person in general. A quiz about "Muhammad" invites a verdict on Islam. A quiz about the Constitution of Medina invites an argument about a document. The first is not a court case; the second is exactly what this hall is for.

A HALT COSTS NOTHING.
A FRAME THAT WOUNDS COSTS THE HALL ITS RIGHT TO WEIGH ANYTHING.

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · THE SACRED FRAMES · ruled · 2026-07-19 · Rev. B

THE STACK · Amenti
· 

Specification·The Shelf·Ingram Manor LLC

19 July 2026·2026-07-19·Rev. A

Amenti.live · Many Quizzes, One Figure

TheSTACK

One Card · Several Gates · Three Depths

The court was built with the figure permanent and the charges plural. The quiz surface was not. Running the engine twice on Caesar today does not deepen his card — it produces a second Caesar card, with the same face and the same stat line, standing beside the first.

THE ARENA RENDERS ONE CARD PER QUIZ.
THE MOMENT A FIGURE HAS TWO, THE ROSTER IS WRONG.

The blocker, drawn

Figure 1 · what happens today, and what should happen instead.

This is not a polish problem. It is the reason the second quiz cannot simply be written: there is nowhere for it to go. Four things stand between here and a stack, and only one of them is a schema change.

# What is missing Where 

1 The roster groups by quiz, not by figure amenti-roster.js 

2 Nothing records order — no depth, no sequence topics table 

3 The engine repeats itself, given the same name amenti-quizzard 

4 A card points at one fixed quiz data-topic 

Number three is already proven rather than predicted. Einstein, run twice from identical input, produced einstein-annus-mirabilis and einstein-miracle-year — the same moment under two ids. The engine has no memory of what it has already written, so left alone it will write the Rubicon four times.

Depth — the dimension the stack needs

Figure 2 · three depths, and what each is for.

A stack without ordering is a pile. Depth is one column and it does three jobs: it tells the card what to offer next, it tells the engine what kind of quiz to write, and it tells a returning seeker how far in they are.

Depth What it is How many Mode 

1 · entry the moment everyone half-knows exactly one per figure life 

2 · the life the other charges — Gaul, the dictatorship, the Ides as many as the life holds life or canon 

3 · contested his own words, and where the sources disagree as many as the corpus holds source 

Depth 3 is where the platform earns its hair-splitters, and it is the depth that needs something the others do not: a source per claim rather than a bibliography per quiz. At depth 1 the question is what happened. At depth 3 the question is which source says what — and Suetonius and Plutarch do not agree about the sentence Caesar spoke at the river.

Source mode already has this property and the other two do not. Its passage is string-matched against the file it came from, so every key is provably in a specific text. That is why depth 3 belongs to source mode and why the reading rooms turned out to be the deep end rather than a nice addition.

The build

Stack IGive the Library an Orderfirst · one column

One column, one backfill. Everything else depends on it and nothing depends on the rest.

alter table topics
add column if not exists depth smallint not null default 1,
add column if not exists figure text; -- facets.figure[0], denormalised

update topics
set figure = payload->'facets'->'figure'->>0
where figure is null;

create index if not exists topics_figure_idx on topics (figure, depth);

figure is denormalised deliberately. It already exists inside the payload, but grouping a roster by a JSON path on every read is a query nobody will enjoy maintaining, and the value never changes once written.

Done when
Every live quiz has a figure and a depth, and select figure, count(*) from topics group by figure shows exactly which figures already have more than one.

Stack IIOne Card Per Figurethe roster

/quiz/topics gains figure and depth. The roster groups by figure and renders one card carrying a list of gates rather than a single data-topic.

What the card becomes

The face, the era and the stat bars are unchanged — they belong to the figure and were always the same across a stack. What changes is beneath them: instead of one row of six marks, a short list of gates, each with its own marks and its own state.

passed every mark struck · emerald · re-enterable but nothing left to earn 

in progress some marks struck · gold · the card's default action 

locked the gate before it is not open · dim · shown, never hidden 

Locked gates are visible. A seeker should be able to see that Caesar holds four charges before they have earned the right to the second — that is the invitation. A hidden gate persuades nobody.

Done when
Caesar has four quizzes and the arena shows one Caesar.

Stack IIITeach the Engine Not to Repeatalready proven broken

Before writing, the engine reads what already exists for that figure and is told plainly what it may not do again.

GET /quizzard/run { figure: "Julius Caesar", depth: 2 }

1 read topics where figure = 'Julius Caesar'
2 pass the existing titles and pivots into the prompt
3 "These moments are ALREADY WRITTEN. Choose a different one.
If the life holds no further pivot worth a quiz, HALT with
reason no-further-pivot."

The halt matters as much as the instruction. Most figures do not hold four quizzes. A minor figure has one moment, and an engine that cannot say that is all there is will manufacture a second by splitting the first — which is how a stack becomes padding.

Done when
Caesar run twice returns two different moments, and a one-moment figure run twice halts with no-further-pivot.

Stack IVSource Per Claimdepth 3 only

At depth 3 the question is which source says what, so the citation has to travel with the claim rather than with the quiz. That means an optional cite on a question, and a passage that names its text.

Source mode is already there — its passage is verifiably from one file. What it does not yet do is let a single quiz draw on two texts and ask a seeker to weigh them against each other, which is the depth-3 question in its purest form.

Decide when this is reached, not before

· Can one quiz quote two works? The verbatim check would then run per passage rather than per quiz — a small change with a large consequence for what depth 3 can ask.

· Does a disagreement between sources become a philosophical pan? "Make the strongest case that Suetonius is the better witness" is a real question and an unusual one.

What it does to the Gates

Nothing is invalidated. THE GATES governs the marks within one quiz — four hearts, two quills, the gate opens. This governs what lies between quizzes.

The one place they meet: the next gate opens when the previous one does. Six marks on caesar-rubicon unlocks caesar-gaul. That is the whole progression, and it needs no new state — the progress endpoint already returns gateOpen per topic.

A STACK IS NOT MORE QUIZZES ABOUT A MAN.
IT IS THE SAME MAN, ASKED HARDER.

The order

# Move Touches Ships alone? 

1 depth + figure columns, backfilled the table yes · changes nothing visible 

2 /quiz/topics returns them mint · read only yes 

3 roster groups by figure the client yes · works with one quiz each 

4 engine reads what exists, refuses to repeat amenti-quizzard yes 

5 write the second quizzes nothing new the payoff 

6 source per claim, depth 3 engine + schema later 

Steps 1 to 3 are safe in any order and visible to nobody until a figure actually has two quizzes. They can go in this week. Step 4 is the one that makes the second quiz possible at all — and until it exists, running the engine twice on the same figure produces the same moment twice, as Einstein already demonstrated.

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · THE STACK · specification · 2026-07-19 · Rev. A

  BRIEF 28 OF 50  ·  AMENTI.LIVE — THE TRIANGLE: HOW THE VOICE IS MADE

  written  2026-07-18
