THE LEIF · Construction Document · Amenti
· 

Construction Document·
The Shelf·
Ingram Manor LLC

18 July 2026·
2026-07-18·
Rev. B

Amenti.live · The Vessel of the Quizzard

TheLEIF

Construction Document · The Quiz Ship

The vessel that carries the Quizzard to sea. Three parts: a hull that remembers, an engine that produces, and rigging that sails her unattended.

Why she carries this name

Leif Erikson holds no reading room. He has one row in names.csv — a rank, a title, two dates, a paragraph. By every assumption we held before tonight, that was not enough to build a quiz from. Depth came first; the library was the prerequisite; a figure earned a quiz by having a room.

That assumption was tested and it broke. Leif's quiz is complete, verified, and indistinguishable in quality from Lincoln's — who has the full reading room, the voice, the archive. The substance never came from the room. It came from the research.

The consequence is the whole reason this ship exists: quiz production is decoupled from library depth. The two resources grow on independent timelines. Breadth can run years ahead of depth, and neither waits on the other. A man who reached a new world with nothing but what he already carried is the right name for the vessel that does the same.

HE REACHED A NEW WORLD WITH WHAT HE ALREADY CARRIED.

SO DOES SHE. THE ROW IS THE ANCHOR — THE RESEARCH IS THE VOYAGE.

What she is, and what she is not

THE QUIZZARD is the specification — it says what a quiz must be: the six steps, the invariant, the halt conditions, the duality. It is law, and it does not change often.

THE LEIF is the machine that executes it — she says how the ship is built: the tables, the Worker, the schedule, the sea trials. Where the two disagree, the Quizzard wins; she is the standing order, and the ship serves it.

Part I
The Hull
The Queue That Remembers

A ship without a hull is a pile of timber. The hull here is memory — the record of which of the 893 have been attempted, which produced a quiz, which were refused and why. Without it the station re-walks ground it has already covered, re-hits halts it has already learned, and no one can ever say how far the voyage has come.

The record — one row per figure

create table quizzard_queue (
figure_name text primary key, -- from names.csv, exact
rank numeric, -- the working order
status text not null, -- pending | done | halted
topic_id text, -- 'leif-vinland' when done
halt_reason text, -- which gate refused, verbatim
sources jsonb, -- the URLs verification rested on
attempted_at timestamptz,
updated_at timestamptz default now()
);

Seeded from
names.csv, deny-filtered, dead only — death-date is not the 2026 placeholder.

Pre-marked done
The 8 live topics and the 5 authored by hand. The ship does not redo finished work.

Working order
By rank ascending. The best-known figures first — they carry the most interest per unit of work.

Halts are kept
A refusal is a finding, not a blank. The reason is recorded so the pool's real shape becomes visible.

The halt log is the most under-rated artifact in this build. After two hundred rows it will tell you, with evidence, exactly what the roster is made of — how many legends, how many empty pans, how many names that resolve to no one. That is knowledge no amount of planning could have produced.

Sea trial · Part I

Run the seeding twice. The second run must change nothing — no duplicate rows, no re-attempted figures, no lost halt reasons. A hull that leaks state is not a hull.

How the hull fails

· No memory — the station re-researches the same figure forever, burning tokens and time on finished ground.

· Halts discarded — the same refusal is re-discovered every pass; the pool never becomes legible.

· Declared, not observed — a manifest that states what should be there rather than what is. The fleet has been bitten by this before: when the brief and the ship disagree, the ship wins.

Part II
The Engine
amenti-quizzard · The Worker

The engine takes one row and returns either a quiz or a refusal. It never returns a guess. It runs the six steps of the Quizzard with the specification itself as its standing order, and it is gated by a validator it cannot talk its way past.

The passage of one row

POST /quizzard/run { figure_name }

1 IDENTIFY resolve to one person ......... else HALT ambiguous
2 RESEARCH search; find the pivot ........ else HALT no-pivot
3 VERIFY confirm every future key ...... else HALT unverifiable
4 DUALITY the two honest cases .......... else HALT empty-pan
5 COMPOSE fill the fixed schema
6 VALIDATE machine-check the invariant ... else RE-RUN (max 2)

-> { status:'done', topic: {...}, sources:[...] }
-> { status:'halted', reason:'empty-pan', note:'...' }

The validator — the gate that cannot be charmed

Step 6 is not a review; it is a parser. A quiz that fails any line is rejected before a human ever sees it:

JSON parses ✓
questions.length === 6 ✓
cognitive 3 what · 1 why · 2 evaluate ✓
answerType 3 mc · 1 cloze · 2 philosophical ✓
timed === 2, on easy recall MC only ✓
passage word count 115–155 ✓
every mc has 4 options and a correct index ✓
cloze has a non-empty accepted[] array ✓
both philosophical carry wordLimit + rubric ✓
topic_id is kebab-case and not already taken ✓

Isolation
The engine holds a model key and a search key. It therefore never shares a Worker with the mint. Writes where the money is stay where the money is.

Answer keys
Emitted server-side only, into TOPICS. A key that reaches the page is a key that is already broken.

Sources retained
Every URL the verification rested on is stored with the quiz. A claim without a source is a claim that cannot be defended later.

Refusal is output
A halt returns cleanly with its reason. The engine that cannot say no will eventually say something false.

Sea trial · Part II — the Leif test

Feed the engine Leif Erikson's row and nothing else — no library, no reading room, no hint. It must return a quiz that stands beside the hand-authored leif-vinland: same shape, verified facts, an honest explorer's duality. Then feed it Homer. It must refuse. Both results are required to pass. A ship that only knows how to say yes is not seaworthy.

Why Homer must be refused

The refusal is not because Homer is unimportant — he may be the most consequential name in Western letters. It is because there is no verifiable life to build a quiz from.

The trouble has a name: the Homeric Question, one of the oldest unresolved debates in scholarship. Was Homer a single poet, a succession of them, or simply a name later attached to an oral tradition that had been evolving for centuries? Antiquity never doubted him; from the eighteenth century onward, scholars did. The modern position is candid — Homer is the presumed author, a formulation that openly admits we do not know, and may never know.

Now run him through the gates and watch where he stops:

Gate What it asks Homer 

01 Identify One real person? Contested. One poet, many, or none. 

02 Research A pivotal moment? The blind bard of Chios is tradition, not record. 

03 Verify Facts that become keys? HALT. No biographical evidence exists. Ancient sources contradict each other on when he lived. 

Every answer key for a Homer quiz would be a legend asserted as fact — and a seeker who knows the scholarship would be marked wrong for knowing better. That is precisely the failure the whole ship is built to prevent.

The distinction worth keeping: the ship refuses the biography, not the subject. The poems are real, their influence is real, and the Homeric Question itself is a genuine, argue-able controversy. A future quiz on sourceType: "Idea" could put the question on the scale — one poet or a tradition — without ever asserting a life that cannot be verified. The halt closes one door and leaves another honestly open.

How the engine fails

· Confident fabrication — the catastrophic failure. A quiz that reads perfectly and is wrong poisons the ledger and the trust behind it.

· Verification skipped under load — the speed limit is the research. An engine that outruns it is not faster, only wronger.

· Halts suppressed — a run tuned to always produce will produce anything.

· Keys leaked to the client — the quiz becomes a formality and the economy becomes free money.

Part III
The Rigging
The Patrol · Sailing Unattended

The hull remembers and the engine produces, but both still need a hand on them. The rigging is what lets her sail without one — the schedule, the staging, and the gate that decides what reaches the live docket.

The watch

cron · a small number of rows per run, not a flood
take N pending from quizzard_queue, ordered by rank
run each through the engine
done -> append to a STAGING file, mark the row
halted -> record the reason, mark the row
publish a run summary the Probe Corps can read

Staged, not live
Early runs land in staging. The captain merges into TOPICS. The gate loosens only once the quizzes have earned it.

Small batches
A handful per run. A ship that produces two hundred bad quizzes overnight is worse than one that produces none.

Visible refusals
The halt log surfaces on a pane. The Glass Gate applies: show the real reading or the empty box, never a comfortable silence.

Probeable
The Corps gets a probe for her — does the queue advance, do halts carry reasons, does staging match what the engine claims it wrote?

The loosening of the gate

Stage Human role Earned by 

Attended Reviews every quiz before merge the first runs 

Spot-checked Reviews a sample; the rest merge ~50 clean quizzes 

Exception-only Reviews halts and flagged output only a sustained clean record 

Open sea Reads the summary. The ship sails. the long quiet 

Sea trial · Part III

One week, unattended, small batches. At the end: the docket has grown, every halt carries a reason a stranger could understand, and not one fabricated fact has reached the live TOPICS. If the docket grew but a single quiz cannot be traced to its sources, the quiz was a failure regardless of the count.

How the rigging fails

· Volume mistaken for progress — the count climbs while quality quietly falls. Quizzes are not tonnage.

· The gate opened too early — autonomy granted before it was earned by evidence.

· A silent patrol — running but reporting nothing, so no one knows whether it works. Being loaded is not being used.

· Nobody watching the watch — the Corps has no probe on her, so her first failure is discovered by a user.

The launch sequence

# Milestone Done when 

1 The hull is laid — queue table seeded, 13 marked done seeding is idempotent 

2 The engine on the bench — one row, hand-fed Leif passes · Homer refused 

3 Engine mated to hull — a batch of ten 10 attempted, states correct 

4 Rigged — cron on, staged output, halt log published a week unattended, clean 

5 Open sea — the gate loosened by record the docket grows unwatched 

Her standing orders

Carried from the fleet's law, and binding on every part of this vessel:

· Verify before the key. No answer is written from memory. Ever.

· A halt is a success. The refusal that says cannot verify is the ship working. The confident fabrication is the wreck.

· The keys never leave the Worker. A key on the page is an economy already lost.

· When the brief and the ship disagree, the ship wins. Measure the vessel, do not trust the drawing.

· The captain is not a component. Every stage that still requires him is a stage not yet finished.

THE HULL REMEMBERS. THE ENGINE PRODUCES. THE RIGGING SAILS HER.

AND ONE DAY SHE WORKS THE DEAD WHILE NO ONE IS WATCHING.

Amenti.live · Ingram Manor LLC · THE LEIF · construction document · companion to THE QUIZZARD · 2026-07-18 · Rev. B

THE SLIPWAY · Implementation Plan · Amenti
· 

Implementation Plan·
The Shelf·
Ingram Manor LLC

18 July 2026·
2026-07-18·
Rev. B

Amenti.live · Where the Leif Gets Built

TheSLIPWAY

Implementation Plan · Six Phases

Three briefs describe a machine. This one says how it actually gets built, in what order, by whose hands — and how you know each stage worked before the next begins.

The honest constraint

Every plan that ignores who can do what is fiction. This one is shaped around the real situation, which is unusual and worth stating plainly.

The captain can

Deploy

· Upload whole files through the GitHub web editor

· Run SQL in the Supabase editor

· Create and deploy Cloudflare Workers, set secrets

· Verify anything by opening it

The assistant cannot

Reach anything

· Push to a repository

· Call a live endpoint or read a private table

· Deploy a Worker or run the engine

· See whether a deployed thing works

So every phase below reduces to the same shape: here is a file, paste it in this place, and here is the one check that proves it worked. Nothing in this plan requires the captain to write code — only to move files and read results.

A PHASE IS NOT DONE WHEN THE FILE IS UPLOADED.

IT IS DONE WHEN ITS ACCEPTANCE TEST PASSES. BEING LOADED IS NOT BEING USED.

Phase 0Land What Is Already Builtdo first

Five verified quizzes are written, validated, and sitting in staging: confucius-lu, manlius-capitol, keller-water, rand-atlas, leif-vinland. They are doing nothing. Landing them takes the economy from 8 topics to 13 — a sixty percent increase in its entire foundation — with no new code whatsoever.

Assistant builds
The merged TOPICS block, five quizzes inserted into the existing structure without disturbing the eight that are live.

Captain deploys
One paste into the amenti-mint Worker, then deploy.

Blocked on one thing

The assistant no longer holds the current amenti-mint source, and will not guess at a file that handles money. The captain pastes the worker source; the merged file comes back the same session. This is the only blocker in the entire plan, and it costs one message.

Acceptance

/quiz/topics returns 13. Each new quiz opens, scores, and credits ET. The eight originals are untouched.

Phase 1Topics Leave the Codethe scale gate

Today every quiz lives inside the money Worker as a hardcoded object. At thirteen that is merely awkward. At three hundred it is untenable — and it means every new quiz requires editing and redeploying the one Worker that holds the service key. That is a bad place to be doing routine content work.

The move

create table topics (
topic_id text primary key,
payload jsonb not null, -- the whole quiz, keys included
mode text default 'life',-- life | canon
status text default 'live',-- live | staged
sources jsonb,
created_at timestamptz default now()
);
-- NOT publicly readable. The mint Worker reads it with the service key.
-- Answer keys never leave the Worker. Nothing about that changes.

Assistant builds
The table SQL, a migration inserting all 13 existing quizzes, and the revised mint Worker that reads the table instead of the constant.

Captain deploys
Run the SQL, paste the revised Worker, deploy, test a quiz end to end.

This is the one phase that touches the money Worker, so it is the one to be slowest about. Verify /quiz/start and /quiz/submit both still work before moving on. If anything is wrong, the old Worker is one paste away — keep a copy.

Acceptance

A new quiz can be added by inserting one row — with no Worker redeploy at all. That is the gate everything after this depends on.

Phase 2The Engine on the Benchthe real unlock

Until now every quiz has come out of a conversation. This phase ends that. A new isolated Worker takes one name and returns a validated quiz or an honest refusal.

How verification actually happens

The engine calls the Anthropic API with the web search tool enabled, carrying the Quizzard specification as its standing order. This is the load-bearing detail of the whole build: a model writing from memory is precisely the fabrication risk every brief was written to prevent. The search tool is what makes Step 3 real rather than decorative.

Worker: amenti-quizzard (isolated — never the mint)
Secret: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (Worker secret; never in a repo)

POST /quizzard/run { figure_name, mode }
system prompt = THE QUIZZARD spec (+ THE CANNONS when mode=canon)
tools = [ web_search ]
-> model researches, verifies, composes
-> VALIDATOR runs in the Worker, not in the model
-> { status:'done', topic:{...}, sources:[...] }
-> { status:'halted', reason:'contested-existence', note:'...' }

Assistant builds
The complete Worker: prompt assembly, the API call with search, JSON extraction, and the full invariant validator with retry.

Captain deploys
Create the Worker in the Cloudflare dashboard, paste, add the secret, deploy. Same path as amenti-terminal.

Acceptance — the Leif Test

Feed it Leif Erikson and nothing else: it must return a quiz that stands beside the hand-authored one. Then feed it Homer: it must refuse, with a reason. Both results are required. A ship that only knows how to say yes is not seaworthy.

What it will cost

One quiz is one model call with a handful of searches. The honest answer is that nobody knows the real figure yet — measure it on the first ten and multiply. The order of magnitude is cents per quiz, which puts a full roster in the tens-to-low-hundreds of dollars, not thousands. Worth confirming before running nine hundred.

Phase 3The Hull — Queue and Stagingmemory

The engine works but forgets. This phase gives it memory, a work order, and a holding pen so nothing reaches the live docket unseen.

Assistant builds
quizzard_queue SQL plus a seed file generated from names.csv — dead only, deduped, ordered by rank, the 13 pre-marked done.

Assistant builds
A /quizzard/batch endpoint that takes N pending rows and writes results to topics with status='staged'.

Captain deploys
Run the SQL, redeploy the quizzard Worker, run a batch of ten.

The halt log is the quiet prize here. After two hundred rows it will describe the roster with evidence — how many legends, how many empty pans, how many names resolving to nobody. That is knowledge no amount of planning could have produced, and it is the input to every later decision about the pool.

Acceptance

Ten attempted, states correct, halts carry readable reasons — and a second run repeats none of the work.

Phase 4The Rigging — The Patrolunattended

The cron. Small batches on a schedule, output staged, refusals visible. The point at which the ship stops needing a hand on her.

Assistant builds
The cron handler, the run-summary format, and a Fleet-Documents pane showing produced / halted / pending with reasons.

Assistant builds
A probe for the Corps: does the queue advance, do halts carry reasons, does staging match what the engine claims it wrote?

Captain deploys
Enable the cron, upload the pane, watch for a week.

The gate loosens by evidence, never by impatience

Stage Human role Earned by 

Attended Reviews every quiz before promotion the first runs 

Spot-checked Reviews a sample; the rest promote ~50 clean quizzes 

Exception-only Reviews halts and flags a sustained record 

Open sea Reads the summary the long quiet 

Acceptance

One week unattended. The docket grew, every halt is legible to a stranger, and no unsourced claim reached a live quiz. If the count rose but one quiz cannot be traced to its sources, the week failed regardless of the count.

Phase 5Run Out the Cannonscaptain first

Canon mode. The mythic band, the legendary founders, the scriptures — roughly a hundred and fifty quizzes in the highest-recognition part of the roster, and the layer that does the actual teaching.

Gated on the captain, by design

The Cannons brief withholds one thing from the engine: no generator improvises a duality for a religious figure. Those frames are written by the captain and supplied to the engine. Canon mode does not run until they exist. This is the single place in the fleet where autonomy is deliberately refused.

Captain writes
The interpretive frames for religious figures. A short list — the classes, not every name.

Assistant builds
Canon mode in the engine: the register rule, citation-per-key enforcement, and a validator that rejects a passage asserting a myth as history.

Acceptance

A canon quiz produced with a citation on every key. Then a deliberate register breach — a passage stating flatly that Hippolyta ruled at Themiscyra — must be rejected by the validator, not caught by a human.

The critical path

Everything above, reduced to what actually gates what:

# Move Unblocks 

1 Captain pastes the amenti-mint source → five quizzes merged and deployed 8 → 13 today 

2 Decide whether topics move from code to a table all volume 

3 Build and bench the engine; run the Leif Test production without a chat window 

4 Queue, staging, patrol unattended growth 

5 Captain writes the sacred frames the mythic band 

Decisions the assistant should not make alone

· Does TOPICS move to a table? Necessary for volume, and it touches the Worker that handles money. The risk is real and the timing is yours.

· What model, and what budget ceiling? Measure ten quizzes, then set a cap the Worker enforces.

· How long does review stay mandatory? The table above proposes evidence thresholds; the numbers are a suggestion, not a law.

· The sacred frames. Captain only. No delegation, by design.

What could go wrong

Risk Guard 

Breaking the live economy while touching the mint Worker keep the old file; verify start+submit before proceeding 

Cost runs away unmeasured measure ten; cap in the Worker 

Model returns malformed JSON validator + bounded retry; a failure is a halt, not a guess 

Quality drifts once volume rises staging, spot checks, sources on every claim 

The API key ends up in a public repo Worker secret only — never in a file that gets uploaded 

Building the whole ship before proving one quiz Phase 0 first. It needs no new code and moves the number today. 

PHASE 0 COSTS ONE MESSAGE AND RAISES THE FOUNDATION BY SIXTY PERCENT.

EVERYTHING ELSE IS CONSTRUCTION. START THERE.
